---
name: aster-code-review
description: Review code against Asterinas's persona-keyed coding guidelines and write a Markdown review file. Use when asked to review a Git change (diff mode) or a set of target files (files mode) for defects, or inside a write-test-review loop.
---

# aster-code-review

Review code against Asterinas's coding guidelines
— the persona-keyed pages under `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/`
— and write one Markdown review file.
The review is **recall-first**: miss as few real defects as possible.
It makes both objective calls (undeniable bugs) and subjective calls (guideline violations, each grounded in a cited rule).

There are two review modes, both **anchored at the current checkout (HEAD)**:

- **`diff <base>`** — a Git change:
  the **commit series** the branch adds over its merge-base with `<base>`
  — each commit's message *and* its diff,
  so the review covers per-commit intent and commit hygiene, not just the net change.
  (Commit first; uncommitted edits are not reviewed in diff mode.)
- **`files <path[:lines] ...>`**
  — target code: the working-tree contents of the named files,
  optionally narrowed to line ranges.

To review at a specific commit, check it out first (it becomes HEAD).

This skill is agent-agnostic (Claude Code and Codex).
Only step 3's spawn primitive differs between agents;
everything else is identical.

## Interface

However the skill is triggered,
it receives ONE raw argument string that begins with a mode word:

```
diff   <base>              <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
files  <path[:lines] ...>  <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
```

- `<base>` (diff) — **required**,
  any ref or SHA;
  reviews the commit series `merge-base(<base>, HEAD)..HEAD` (each commit's message + diff).
- `<path[:lines] ...>` (files) — **required**, one or more targets in the working tree.
  A target is a path, optionally `path:N-M` or `path:N-M,K-L` (1-based, inclusive);
  repeat a path to add ranges.
  Wrap a path in double quotes to allow spaces or colons (`"a: b.rs":10-20`).
- `<output>` — **required, last positional** (`cp src... dest` style);
  refuse to overwrite unless `--overwrite`.
- `--overwrite` — replace the output file if it already exists.
- `--per-persona-context` — `yes`: fan out one isolated agent per persona (best recall).
  `no`: one combined agent reviews all personas (cheaper, lower recall).
  `auto` (default) currently resolves to `yes`;
  it will become a benchmark-driven heuristic.
  Controls step 3 only.

**Do not pre-split the argument string yourself.**
Pass it verbatim, as a single quoted argument,
to `resolve_target.sh` (step 1)
— the script self-tokenizes (whitespace-split except inside double quotes),
which keeps the parse deterministic and identical across agents.

## Pipeline

Run these steps in order.
Steps 1 and 5 are deterministic scripts;
steps 6–8 use the model.

The helper scripts live in the skill's own `scripts/` directory,
at `.agents/skills/aster-code-review/scripts/` inside the repository under review.
**Invoke every script by its absolute path**
— the working directory is the code under review (the repo root), *not* the skill directory,
so a bare `scripts/…` is not found (notably on Codex, which runs commands from the repo root; do not `cd` into the skill dir either — `resolve_target.sh` needs the repo as its cwd).
Shells do not persist between commands,
so **set `SKILL` at the start of each command that uses it** (or inline the `$(git rev-parse …)`):

```sh
SKILL="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.agents/skills/aster-code-review"; "$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'
```

The steps below write `$SKILL/scripts/…` as shorthand for that absolute path.

1. **Resolve the target.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'` prints the canonical review input
   — the commit series (each commit's message + diff) in `diff` mode,
   annotated file excerpts in `files` mode;
   save it to a temp file.
   Run it again with `--meta` first (`"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" --meta '<raw args>'`) to get
   `mode=`, `base=`/`files=`, `head=`, `branch=`, `output=`, `overwrite=`, `per_persona_context=`;
   add `date=` (today) and an optional `title=` to make the meta file.
   Pass the raw argument string as a single quoted argument
   so the script's tokenizer sees it intact.
2. **Activate personas.**
   Pick which of the five personas run,
   from the reviewed paths — changed paths in `diff` mode,
   named paths in `files` mode (see *Activation*).
3. **Fan out.**
   Spawn the persona passes (see *Spawning*).
   With `per_persona_context` = `yes` or `auto` (the default),
   run **one isolated PASS per activated persona** — best recall.
   With `no`, run **one combined PASS** over all activated personas in a single context
   — cheaper, lower recall.
   Build each pass's prompt deterministically with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
   — one persona for fan-out, all activated personas for combined
   — and spawn the sub-agent with **that exact text**.
   Each pass returns a JSON array of comments;
   file each under its persona's `<fragdir>/<persona>.json` (the comment's `persona` field says which),
   so step 5 is unchanged.
4. **Collect** the per-persona JSON fragments.
5. **Assemble.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/assemble_review.sh" [--overwrite] <meta> <fragdir> <output>` performs the deterministic merge (group by persona in fixed order, sort by file→line, drop exact duplicates *within a persona*, write frontmatter, leave a `<!-- SUMMARY -->` placeholder).
   Pass `--overwrite` only if the user gave it;
   otherwise the script refuses to clobber an existing `<output>`.
6. **Verify** (see *Verification*).
7. **Consolidate** (see *Consolidation*).
8. **Summary.**
   Replace `<!-- SUMMARY -->` with a constructive summary over the final comments:
   what the code does well, the top issues ranked by severity,
   any structural recommendations.

## Activation

A persona runs unless the reviewed paths *provably* contain nothing in its remit (reviewed paths = changed paths in `diff` mode, named paths in `files` mode):

- **maintainability, development, security** — any reviewed code.
- **hardware** — a reviewed path is assembly (`*.S`, `*.asm`),
  an architecture directory (`.../arch/...`), or contains `asm!` / `global_asm!`.
- **documentation** — a reviewed path is `book/`, any `*.md`,
  syscall-coverage files (`*.scml`), or a user-facing API surface (a syscall or kernel parameter).

Activation is path-based and deterministic;
do not use a model to triage (a wrongly-skipped persona is a silent recall hole).

## Pass contract

The shared reviewer contract
— the review methodology and the JSON comment schema every pass must follow
— lives in [`scripts/pass_contract.md`](scripts/pass_contract.md),
inlined as the **stable head** of every pass prompt by `build_pass_prompt.sh` (step 3).
Do not paraphrase it into the prompt yourself:
retyping it breaks the byte-identical prefix the prompt cache relies on (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).
A pass reads only the persona block(s) it is given (selective exposure),
reviews the **REVIEW INPUT** at the foot of the prompt,
and returns a JSON array of comments per that schema.

## Spawning a pass

Build every pass prompt with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
and spawn the sub-agent with its **exact** output
— only a script keeps the prompt's stable head (contract + guideline) byte-identical across passes and reviews,
which is what lets the prompt cache reuse it (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).

**Default (`per_persona_context` = `yes`/`auto`)**
— one pass per activated persona,
each in a CLEAN context with only its own persona block (selective exposure):

- **Claude Code** — spawn a Task sub-agent per persona.
- **Codex** — run `codex exec "<build_pass_prompt.sh output>"` per persona
  (pass the built PROMPT TEXT as the argument).

**Never spawn a pass by re-running `aster_code_review.sh` / `run_agent.sh`,
nor by re-issuing the skill's own arguments** (e.g. `codex exec … diff <base> <out>`).
A pass is a *reviewer* invocation whose input is the `build_pass_prompt.sh` text
— not another run of this skill.
Re-entering the launcher spawns another orchestrator that spawns another … , an infinite fork bomb;
the launcher now refuses it (`ACR_AGENT_RUNNING`).

Passes are independent;
order does not matter.
Collect each pass's JSON.

**Combined (`per_persona_context` = `no`)**
— build ONE prompt with all activated personas (`"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input> <p1> <p2> ...`)
and run a single pass;
it reviews through every lens at once and tags each comment with its `persona`.
The input and spawn overhead are paid once,
so it is cheaper
— but a larger shared context dilutes focus,
so it finds fewer defects.
The default stays fan-out:
recall comes first,
and `no` is an explicit, opt-in concession.

## Verification (step 6)

For each comment, isolate the key premise it rests on
— especially an external-system fact (Linux/POSIX behaviour, the System V ABI, Rust semantics).
Try to **refute** it:
re-read the cited code and consult an authoritative source.
Assign a verdict:

- **confirmed** — keep the comment unchanged.
- **uncertain** — keep it, but prefix `problem` with `(unverified) `.
- **refuted** — remove the comment,
  and append it to a `## Retracted by verification` list at the foot of the file with a one-line reason.

Remove **only** on confident refutation;
an unsure check is `uncertain`, not `refuted`.
This is the only step that may remove a comment, and only false positives.

## Consolidation (step 7)

Find clusters of comments that share one root cause or one fix
(e.g. several manual lock/unlock pairs that all want RAII).
For each cluster, write a single unified fix
and repoint each member's `**Fix.**` at it ("Shared with the other `raii` comments: …").
**Never remove a comment**
— every symptom stays at its own location.

## Output format

The assembled file is:
frontmatter (`date`, `mode`, `base` or `files`, `head`, `branch`, optional `title`),
a `# Summary`,
then a `## <section>` per persona in the order **Maintainability, Correctness, Security, Hardware, Documentation** (Correctness is the Development persona's section).
Each comment is a `### \`file\` line N` subsection:
a quoted ` ```diff ` snippet,
then a prose line `` `<grounding>` (<severity>): <problem> ``,
then a `**Fix.**` paragraph.
Do not restructure what the script emits;
only fill the summary and apply the verify/consolidate edits.

#!/usr/bin/env bash

# SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

#
# assemble_review.sh — deterministic merge of persona comment fragments into the review file body.
# No LLM judgement here:
# group by persona in a fixed order,
# sort by file then line within each group,
# drop exact duplicates *within a persona*,
# write the frontmatter,
# and leave a <!-- SUMMARY --> placeholder for the orchestrator's LLM summary step.
#
# Usage: assemble_review.sh [--overwrite] <meta-file> <frag-dir> <output-file>
#   meta-file: lines `mode=`, `base=` (diff) or `files=` (files), `head=`,
#              `branch=`, `date=`, `title=`
#   frag-dir:  one <persona>.json per persona, each a JSON array of comments:
#       {"file","line","grounding","severity","problem","fix","diff"}
#   --overwrite: replace <output-file> if it already exists (otherwise fail).
set -euo pipefail

overwrite=0
pos=()
for a in "$@"; do
    case "$a" in
        --overwrite) overwrite=1 ;;
        --*) echo "assemble_review.sh: unknown flag: $a" >&2; exit 2 ;;
        *) pos+=("$a") ;;
    esac
done
if [[ ${#pos[@]} -ne 3 ]]; then
    echo "usage: assemble_review.sh [--overwrite] <meta-file> <frag-dir> <output-file>" >&2
    exit 2
fi
meta="${pos[0]}"; fragdir="${pos[1]}"; out="${pos[2]}"

# Refuse to clobber an existing review unless --overwrite (design §3).
if [[ -e "$out" && $overwrite -eq 0 ]]; then
    echo "assemble_review.sh: refusing to overwrite existing $out (pass --overwrite)" >&2
    exit 1
fi

python3 - "$meta" "$fragdir" "$out" <<'PY'
import json, os, re, sys
meta_path, fragdir, out = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]

# A guideline short-name is a lowercase kebab identifier (e.g. `lock-ordering`) and
# renders as code — a citable rule. A bug's grounding is a short plain-language
# description (e.g. "Off by one") and renders as prose, so it never reads as a rule name.
KEBAB = re.compile(r"[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*")
def grounding_tag(g):
    return "`%s`" % g if KEBAB.fullmatch(g) else g

meta = {}
for line in open(meta_path):
    line = line.strip()
    if "=" in line:
        k, v = line.split("=", 1)
        meta[k] = v

# Fixed persona order -> review section title.
# "Correctness" is the Development persona's section (a bare "## Development" reads oddly; see the design doc).
ORDER = [("maintainability", "Maintainability"),
         ("development",      "Correctness"),
         ("security",         "Security"),
         ("hardware",         "Hardware"),
         ("documentation",    "Documentation")]

def load(persona):
    p = os.path.join(fragdir, persona + ".json")
    if not os.path.exists(p):
        return []          # persona not activated / did not run: legitimately absent
    # An EXISTING fragment that is unparseable or not a JSON array means a pass
    # produced broken output. Failing closed (rather than dropping it) is essential:
    # this skill is recall-first, so a silently-discarded fragment is a silent
    # recall hole the benchmark/orchestrator would never notice.
    try:
        data = json.load(open(p))
    except Exception as e:
        sys.stderr.write(f"assemble: FATAL: unparseable fragment {p}: {e}\n")
        sys.exit(2)
    if not isinstance(data, list):
        sys.stderr.write(f"assemble: FATAL: fragment {p} is not a JSON array of comments\n")
        sys.exit(2)
    return data

L = ["---",
     f"date: {meta.get('date','')}",
     f"mode: {meta.get('mode','diff')}"]
if meta.get("base"):
    L.append(f"base: {meta['base']}")
if meta.get("files"):
    L.append(f"files: {meta['files']}")
L += [f"head: {meta.get('head','')}",
      f"branch: {meta.get('branch','')}"]
if meta.get("title"):
    # json.dumps yields a valid YAML double-quoted scalar (escapes embedded quotes).
    L.append("title: " + json.dumps(meta["title"]))
L += ["---", "", "# Summary", "", "<!-- SUMMARY -->", ""]

counts = {}
for persona, title in ORDER:
    # Dedup is per-persona: identical comments from two different personas are both kept,
    # in their own sections (design §4).
    seen = set()
    uniq = []
    for c in load(persona):
        # "Exact" duplicate: identical in EVERY field (a normalized full-object key),
        # so two comments that differ in grounding, severity, or diff are both kept.
        # A partial key would collapse distinct findings — a recall hole.
        key = json.dumps(c, sort_keys=True)
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        uniq.append(c)
    counts[persona] = len(uniq)
    if not uniq:
        continue
    # Sort by file, then line, then a stable text key
    # so co-located comments have a deterministic order regardless of pass output order.
    uniq.sort(key=lambda c: (str(c.get("file", "")),
                             int(c.get("line", 0) or 0),
                             str(c.get("grounding", "")),
                             str(c.get("problem", ""))))
    L += [f"## {title}", ""]
    for c in uniq:
        loc = "`%s`" % c.get("file", "?")
        if c.get("line"):
            loc += " line %s" % c["line"]
        L += [f"### {loc}", ""]
        diff = c.get("diff")
        if diff:
            L.append("> ```diff")
            L += ["> " + dl for dl in str(diff).splitlines()]
            L += ["> ```", ""]
        L.append("%s (%s): %s" % (grounding_tag(c.get("grounding", "issue")),
                                  c.get("severity", "major"),
                                  (c.get("problem", "") or "").strip()))
        L += ["", "**Fix.** %s" % (c.get("fix", "") or "").strip(), ""]

open(out, "w").write("\n".join(L).rstrip() + "\n")
sys.stderr.write("assemble: " + ", ".join(f"{p}={counts[p]}" for p, _ in ORDER) + "\n")
PY

 succeeded in 112ms:
#!/usr/bin/env bash

# SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

#
# build_pass_prompt.sh — assemble a persona pass prompt DETERMINISTICALLY,
# ordered for prompt-cache reuse.
# The STABLE blocks come first and are byte-identical across passes and across reviews:
# the shared reviewer contract,
# then one block per persona (its template + its inlined guideline page).
# The VOLATILE review input comes LAST,
# so everything above it is a reusable cache prefix.
#
# The orchestrator must spawn the pass with this script's EXACT output
# — retyping or paraphrasing it loses the byte-identical prefix, and with it the cache hit.
#
#   one persona  => a fan-out pass   (--per-persona-context=yes/auto)
#   several      => the combined pass (--per-persona-context=no)
#
# Usage: build_pass_prompt.sh <input-file> <persona> [<persona> ...]
set -euo pipefail
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
SKILLDIR="$(cd "$HERE/.." && pwd)"
REPO="$(cd "$SKILLDIR/../../.." && pwd)"   # .agents/skills/aster-code-review -> repo root

# Where to read the guideline pages from.
# Priority:
#   1. explicit ACR_GUIDELINE_ROOT, for tests or external harnesses;
#   2. a guideline snapshot bundled beside the overlaid skill;
#   3. the current repo, for normal in-tree use.
#
# The benchmark cannot rely on the review agent preserving ACR_GUIDELINE_ROOT
# across its own shell commands, so overlay_skill.sh bundles a current snapshot
# into the skill package. This keeps historical worktrees on current guidelines
# without modifying their tracked book/ tree.
if [[ -n "${ACR_GUIDELINE_ROOT:-}" ]]; then
    GROOT="$ACR_GUIDELINE_ROOT"
elif [[ -f "$SKILLDIR/guideline-root" ]]; then
    GROOT="$SKILLDIR/guideline-root"
else
    GROOT="$REPO"
fi

input="${1:-}"; shift || true
[[ -n "$input" && -f "$input" ]] || { echo "build_pass_prompt.sh: a readable <input-file> is required" >&2; exit 2; }
[[ $# -ge 1 ]] || { echo "build_pass_prompt.sh: at least one <persona> is required" >&2; exit 2; }

linked_guideline_pages() { # <guideline-page-path-relative-to-GROOT>
    python3 - "$1" "$GROOT/$1" <<'PY'
import posixpath
import re
import sys

root_page = sys.argv[1]
root_file = sys.argv[2]
base_dir = posixpath.dirname(root_page)
seen = set()

with open(root_file, encoding="utf-8") as file:
    page_text = file.read()

for link in re.findall(r"\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)", page_text):
    link = link.split("#", 1)[0]
    if not link or "://" in link or link.startswith("#"):
        continue
    if link.endswith("/"):
        link += "README.md"
    path = posixpath.normpath(posixpath.join(base_dir, link))
    if not path.startswith("book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/"):
        continue
    if path == root_page or path in seen:
        continue
    seen.add(path)
    print(path)
PY
}

inline_guidelines() { # <guideline-page-path-relative-to-GROOT>
    local gp="$1" gfile="$GROOT/$gp" sub
    [[ -f "$gfile" ]] || return 0

    printf 'Rely on the inlined guideline material below as the authoritative guideline context for this pass. Do not re-read `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/` from the worktree under review for guideline content.\n\n'
    printf -- '--- guideline page (%s) ---\n\n' "$gp"
    cat "$gfile"; printf '\n'

    while IFS= read -r sub; do
        [[ -f "$GROOT/$sub" ]] || continue
        printf '\n--- guideline subpage (%s) ---\n\n' "$sub"
        cat "$GROOT/$sub"; printf '\n'
    done < <(linked_guideline_pages "$gp")
}

# --- STABLE: the shared reviewer contract ------------------------------------
cat "$SKILLDIR/scripts/pass_contract.md"
printf '\n'

# --- STABLE: one block per persona (template + inlined guideline page) --------
for persona in "$@"; do
    pf="$SKILLDIR/personas/$persona.md"
    [[ -f "$pf" ]] || { echo "build_pass_prompt.sh: no such persona: $persona" >&2; exit 2; }
    printf '===== PERSONA: %s =====\n\n' "$persona"
    cat "$pf"; printf '\n'
    gp="$(grep -oE 'book/[A-Za-z0-9/_-]+README\.md' "$pf" | head -1 || true)"
    [[ -n "$gp" ]] && inline_guidelines "$gp"
done

# --- VOLATILE: the review input (LAST; everything above is the cache prefix) ---
printf '===== REVIEW INPUT =====\n\n'
cat "$input"

# Pass contract

You are a reviewer applying the persona guideline(s) included below to the change or files under review
(the **REVIEW INPUT** at the very end of this prompt).
Find as many real defects as possible within the included persona(s)' remit
— runtime correctness for Development,
security/soundness for Security,
ABI/hardware for Hardware,
doc style/currency for Documentation,
and structure/process for Maintainability
— without inventing issues; a false alarm is a real cost.

For each persona block below,
work that persona's concerns in the order its file gives.
For each candidate rule,
read its one-line gist first
and drill into the full rule (its linked subsections) only on a suspected violation.
Stay within the remit of the persona(s) you are given.

Each persona searches only for defects whose failure belongs to that persona.
Do not run a general bug sweep from every persona.
When another persona is the clear natural owner,
do not duplicate that investigation here.
For example,
Maintainability should inspect design shape, readability, naming, layout,
and commit hygiene;
it should not trace runtime permission semantics, Linux/POSIX behavior,
wrong predicates, or data-flow edge cases unless they are evidence of a
maintainability rule violation.

Within each included persona's owned failure modes,
reason about the code even when no explicit guideline names the issue.
Examples include off-by-one and reachable panic for Development,
input-validation or permission-boundary flaws for Security,
ABI/alignment hazards for Hardware,
navigation or currency defects for Documentation,
and structural or process defects for Maintainability.
Ground each non-guideline finding in a short plain-language description of the defect
("Off by one", "Use after free", "Reachable panic", …)
— not the bare word `bug`, and not a coined hyphenated short-name,
which would read as a guideline.
Never stay silent about a real defect that belongs to the included persona
because "no guideline covers it".

Be **adversarial**:
before dismissing a suspected in-scope defect as safe,
state the concrete input or interleaving that would trigger it.
Report an in-scope defect unless you can show that case cannot happen.
"It looks fine" is not a verdict.

The REVIEW INPUT is the unit of review;
you MAY read surrounding code in the working tree for extra context.

## Output

Output **only** a JSON array of comment objects (no prose around it):

```json
[{"file":"path/relative/to/repo.rs","line":42,"persona":"development","grounding":"lock-ordering","severity":"major",
  "problem":"`foo()` takes `b.lock()` while already holding `a.lock()`, the reverse of the `a`-before-`b` order elsewhere — a deadlock",
  "fix":"take `a.lock()` before `b.lock()` here too, matching the rest of the module",
  "diff":"the few relevant lines (a diff hunk, or source lines in files mode)"}]
```

- `persona` — which persona section the comment belongs to (`maintainability`, `development`, `security`, `hardware`, `documentation`);
  used to file the comment under the right section.
  In a single-persona (fan-out) pass it is always that persona.
- `grounding` — what the comment rests on, in one of two forms kept visually distinct:
  when you **cite a guideline**, its short-name
  — a lowercase kebab identifier (e.g. `lock-ordering`), rendered as code;
  when you report a **bug no guideline covers**, a short plain-language description of the defect
  (e.g. "Off by one", "Use after free", "Incorrect cleanup"), rendered as prose.
  Do not coin a hyphenated pseudo-short-name for a bug
  — that reads as a guideline
  — and never use the bare word `bug`,
  which says nothing the reader cannot already see.
- `severity` — **required**,
  one of `critical` (must fix) / `major` (should fix) / `minor` (worth fixing) / `nit` (optional or stylistic).
- `problem` and `fix` are **both required**
  — every comment proposes a remedy.
  They are posted as GitHub-flavored Markdown,
  so wrap every code identifier, path, type, function or variable name, and literal value in backticks
  (`self.len`, `Ordering::Acquire`, `kernel/src/foo.rs`),
  and put any multi-line snippet in `fix` in a fenced ```` ``` ```` block.
  (The `grounding` of a bug stays plain prose, as described above
  — only `problem` and `fix` take inline code.)
- Every field above is required on every comment,
  with a single exception for `line`, described next.
- `line` is the line the comment anchors to
  — the post-change line in the commit's diff (`diff` mode),
  or the file's line number (`files` mode).
  It is **required for a finding about code**.
- For a finding about a **commit message** (`diff` mode shows each commit's message),
  set `file` to the commit locus (e.g. `commit abc1234 message`),
  **omit `line`** (its one exception),
  and ground it in a commit-hygiene rule (`imperative-subject`, `atomic-commits`, …).
- Report only issues within the included persona(s)' remit.
  If you find nothing, output `[]`.

===== PERSONA: documentation =====

# Documentation persona

**Review section:** Documentation
**Remit:** Are user-facing docs and compatibility artifacts correct, current, and well-written?

**Your guideline page (read only this, drill in on suspicion):**
`book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/for-documentation/README.md`
— subsections: `general-style.md`, `path-specific/kernel.md`.

**Concerns, in order:**

1. **General style** — `semantic-line-breaks` (break prose at sentence/clause boundaries),
   `readme-as-crate-doc` (a published crate's `README.md` is its crate-level doc).
2. **Path-specific doc currency**
   — `linux-compat-docs`: when a change under `kernel/` alters a user-visible API (a syscall or a kernel parameter),
   the Linux Compatibility docs (Syscall Flag Coverage + `.scml`, or Kernel Parameters) must be updated in the same change.
   A code change that should have a matching doc update but does not is a defect you own.

You activate when the change touches docs, coverage files, or a user-facing API surface.
You own doc correctness and currency, not code behaviour.

Rely on the inlined guideline material below as the authoritative guideline context for this pass. Do not re-read `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/` from the worktree under review for guideline content.

--- guideline page (book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/for-documentation/README.md) ---

# For Documentation

*Are user-facing docs and compatibility artifacts correct, current, and well-written?*

This is the index of the **documentation** guidelines.
Each subsection is its own page,
and each entry below links a stable `short-name` to its guideline,
with a one-line gist so a reader (or a review tool) can grasp the guideline before opening it.

## Index

**[General Style](general-style.md)**
- [`semantic-line-breaks`](general-style.md#semantic-line-breaks): Break prose at sentence/clause boundaries, one idea per line.
- [`readme-as-crate-doc`](general-style.md#readme-as-crate-doc): For a published crate, make `README.md` the crate-level doc (`#![doc = include_str!("../README.md")]`) so crates.io and docs.rs stay in sync.

**[Path-Specific](path-specific/)**
- [`kernel/`](path-specific/kernel.md)
    - [`linux-compat-docs`](path-specific/kernel.md#linux-compat-docs): When a user-visible API (syscall or kernel parameter) changes, update the Linux Compatibility docs (Syscall Flag Coverage + `.scml`, or Kernel Parameters).


--- guideline subpage (book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/for-documentation/general-style.md) ---

# General Style

### Prefer semantic line breaks (`semantic-line-breaks`) {#semantic-line-breaks}

For prose in Markdown and doc comments,
insert line breaks at semantic boundaries
so each line carries one coherent idea.
At minimum, break at sentence boundaries.
For longer sentences, also consider breaking at clause boundaries.

Semantic line breaks make diffs smaller,
reviews easier,
and merge conflicts less noisy.

As an exception,
RFC documents that are mostly read-only
can use regular paragraph wrapping.

See also:
[Semantic Line Breaks](https://sembr.org/).

### Make a crate's `README.md` its crate-level documentation (`readme-as-crate-doc`) {#readme-as-crate-doc}

A published crate's `README.md` (shown on crates.io)
and its crate-level Rust doc (shown on docs.rs)
usually carry the same content.
Keep a single source of truth:
write the `README.md`,
and include it as the crate-level doc rather than maintaining a separate copy.

```rust
#![doc = include_str!("../README.md")]
```

Write the `README.md` so it renders correctly under both a Markdown renderer and rustdoc.

See also:
[Issue #2947](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/issues/2947)
for the rationale, the caveats, and a template;
the [`ostd-pod`](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/tree/main/ostd/libs/ostd-pod) crate
for a crate that already adopts it.


--- guideline subpage (book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/for-documentation/path-specific/README.md) ---

# Path-Specific

Guidelines that apply only to code under a specific repository path.

- [`kernel/`](kernel.md)


--- guideline subpage (book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/for-documentation/path-specific/kernel.md) ---

# `kernel/`

Guidelines that apply only to code under `kernel/`.

### Keep the Linux Compatibility docs current (`linux-compat-docs`) {#linux-compat-docs}

When a change under `kernel/` adds or enhances a user-visible API — a system call or a kernel parameter —
update the [Linux Compatibility](../../../../kernel/linux-compatibility/) section in the same PR,
so the documented coverage keeps matching the code.

- For a **system call**:
  update the matching [Syscall Flag Coverage](../../../../kernel/linux-compatibility/syscall-flag-coverage/) page
  and its [SCML](../../../../kernel/linux-compatibility/syscall-flag-coverage/system-call-matching-language.md) (`.scml`) coverage file
  to reflect the newly supported flags, arguments, and behaviors.
- For a **kernel parameter**:
  update the [Kernel Parameters](../../../../kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md) page.

===== REVIEW INPUT =====

    37	bitflags! {
    38	    #[derive(Default)]
    39	    pub struct CloneFlags: u32 {
    40	        const CLONE_NEWTIME = 0x00000080;       /* New time namespace */
    41	        const CLONE_VM      = 0x00000100;       /* Set if VM shared between processes.  */
    42	        const CLONE_FS      = 0x00000200;       /* Set if fs info shared between processes.  */
    43	        const CLONE_FILES   = 0x00000400;       /* Set if open files shared between processes.  */
    44	        const CLONE_SIGHAND = 0x00000800;       /* Set if signal handlers shared.  */
    45	        const CLONE_PIDFD   = 0x00001000;       /* Set if a pidfd should be placed in parent.  */
    46	        const CLONE_PTRACE  = 0x00002000;       /* Set if tracing continues on the child.  */
    47	        const CLONE_VFORK   = 0x00004000;       /* Set if the parent wants the child to wake it up on mm_release.  */
    48	        const CLONE_PARENT  = 0x00008000;       /* Set if we want to have the same parent as the cloner.  */
    49	        const CLONE_THREAD  = 0x00010000;       /* Set to add to same thread group.  */
    50	        const CLONE_NEWNS   = 0x00020000;       /* Set to create new namespace.  */
    51	        const CLONE_SYSVSEM = 0x00040000;       /* Set to shared SVID SEM_UNDO semantics.  */
    52	        const CLONE_SETTLS  = 0x00080000;       /* Set TLS info.  */
    53	        const CLONE_PARENT_SETTID = 0x00100000; /* Store TID in userlevel buffer before MM copy.  */
    54	        const CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID = 0x00200000;/* Register exit futex and memory location to clear.  */
    55	        const CLONE_DETACHED = 0x00400000;      /* Create clone detached.  */
    56	        const CLONE_UNTRACED = 0x00800000;      /* Set if the tracing process can't force CLONE_PTRACE on this clone.  */
    57	        const CLONE_CHILD_SETTID = 0x01000000;  /* Store TID in userlevel buffer in the child.  */
    58	        const CLONE_NEWCGROUP   = 0x02000000;	/* New cgroup namespace.  */
    59	        const CLONE_NEWUTS	= 0x04000000;	    /* New utsname group.  */
    60	        const CLONE_NEWIPC	= 0x08000000;	    /* New ipcs.  */
    61	        const CLONE_NEWUSER	= 0x10000000;	    /* New user namespace.  */
    62	        const CLONE_NEWPID	= 0x20000000;	    /* New pid namespace.  */
    63	        const CLONE_NEWNET	= 0x40000000;	    /* New network namespace.  */
    64	        const CLONE_IO	= 0x80000000;	        /* Clone I/O context.  */
    65	
    66	        /// A bitmask of all `CloneFlags` related to namespace creation.
    67	        const CLONE_NS_FLAGS = Self::CLONE_NEWTIME.bits() |
    68	            Self::CLONE_NEWNS.bits() |
    69	            Self::CLONE_NEWCGROUP.bits() |
    70	            Self::CLONE_NEWUTS.bits() |
    71	            Self::CLONE_NEWIPC.bits() |
    72	            Self::CLONE_NEWUSER.bits() |
    73	            Self::CLONE_NEWPID.bits() |
    74	            Self::CLONE_NEWNET.bits();
    75	    }
    76	}
    77	
    78	pub(super) fn init() {
    79	    FORKS_COUNTER.call_once(PerCpuCounter::new);
    80	}
    81	
    82	/// An internal structure to homogenize the arguments for `clone` and
    83	/// `clone3`.
    84	///
    85	/// From the clone(2) man page:
    86	///
    87	/// ```
    88	/// The following table shows the equivalence between the arguments
    89	/// of clone() and the fields in the clone_args argument supplied to
    90	/// clone3():
    91	///     clone()         clone3()        Notes
    92	///                     cl_args field
    93	///     flags & ~0xff   flags           For most flags; details
    94	///                                     below
    95	///     parent_tid      pidfd           See CLONE_PIDFD
    96	///     child_tid       child_tid       See CLONE_CHILD_SETTID
    97	///     parent_tid      parent_tid      See CLONE_PARENT_SETTID
    98	///     flags & 0xff    exit_signal
    99	///     stack           stack
   100	///     ---             stack_size
   101	///     tls             tls             See CLONE_SETTLS
   102	///     ---             set_tid         See below for details
   103	///     ---             set_tid_size
   104	///     ---             cgroup          See CLONE_INTO_CGROUP
   105	/// ```
   106	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default)]
   107	pub struct CloneArgs {
   108	    pub flags: CloneFlags,
   109	    pub pidfd: Option<Vaddr>,
   110	    pub child_tid: Vaddr,
   111	    pub parent_tid: Option<Vaddr>,
   112	    pub exit_signal: Option<SigNum>,
   113	    pub stack: u64,
   114	    pub stack_size: Option<NonZeroU64>,
   115	    pub tls: u64,
   116	    pub _set_tid: Option<u64>,
   117	    pub _set_tid_size: Option<u64>,
   118	    pub _cgroup: Option<u64>,
   119	}
   120	
   121	impl CloneArgs {
   122	    /// Prepares a new [`CloneArgs`] based on the arguments for clone(2).
   123	    pub fn for_clone(
   124	        raw_flags: u64,
   125	        parent_tid: Vaddr,
   126	        child_tid: Vaddr,
   127	        tls: u64,
   128	        stack: u64,
   129	    ) -> Result<Self> {
   130	        const FLAG_MASK: u64 = 0xff;
   131	        let flags = CloneFlags::from(raw_flags & !FLAG_MASK);
   132	        if flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS | CloneFlags::CLONE_FS) {
   133	            return_errno_with_message!(
   134	                Errno::EINVAL,
   135	                "CLONE_NEWNS cannot be used with CLONE_FS for clone syscall"
   136	            );
   137	        }
   138	
   139	        let exit_signal = raw_flags & FLAG_MASK;
   140	        // Disambiguate the `parent_tid` parameter. The field is used
   141	        // both for `CLONE_PIDFD` and `CLONE_PARENT_SETTID`, so at
   142	        // most only one can be specified.
   143	        let (pidfd, parent_tid) = match (
   144	            flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PIDFD),
   145	            flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT_SETTID),
   146	        ) {
   147	            (false, false) => (None, None),
   148	            (true, false) => (Some(parent_tid), None),
   149	            (false, true) => (None, Some(parent_tid)),
   150	            (true, true) => {
   151	                return_errno_with_message!(
   152	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   153	                    "CLONE_PIDFD was specified with CLONE_PARENT_SETTID"
   154	                );
   155	            }
   156	        };
   157	
   158	        Ok(Self {
   159	            flags,
   160	            pidfd,
   161	            child_tid,
   162	            parent_tid,
   163	            exit_signal: (exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(exit_signal as u8)),
   164	            stack,
   165	            tls,
   166	            ..Default::default()
   167	        })
   168	    }
   169	
   170	    pub fn for_fork() -> Self {
   171	        Self {
   172	            exit_signal: Some(SIGCHLD),
   173	            ..Default::default()
   174	        }
   175	    }
   176	
   177	    pub fn for_vfork() -> Self {
   178	        Self {
   179	            flags: CloneFlags::CLONE_VFORK | CloneFlags::CLONE_VM,
   180	            exit_signal: Some(SIGCHLD),
   181	            ..Default::default()
   182	        }
   183	    }
   184	
   185	    pub(self) fn check(&self, ctx: &Context) -> Result<()> {
   186	        let clone_flags = self.flags;
   187	        clone_flags.check_unsupported_flags()?;
   188	
   189	        // Reject invalid argument combinations related to the CLONE_PARENT flag.
   190	        if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT) {
   191	            if clone_flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWUSER | CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWPID) {
   192	                return_errno_with_message!(
   193	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   194	                    "`CLONE_PARENT` cannot be used together with `CLONE_NEWUSER` or `CLONE_NEWPID`"
   195	                );
   196	            }
   197	
   198	            if ctx.process.is_init_process() {
   199	                return_errno_with_message!(
   200	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   201	                    "`CLONE_PARENT` cannot be used if the process is the init process"
   202	                )
   203	            }
   204	        }
   205	
   206	        // Reject invalid argument combinations related to the CLONE_THREAD flag.
   207	        if self.flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD) {
   208	            // This combination is not valid, according to the Linux man pages. See
   209	            // <https://www.man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/clone.2.html>.
   210	            if !clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_VM | CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND) {
   211	                return_errno_with_message!(
   212	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   213	                    "`CLONE_THREAD` without `CLONE_VM` and `CLONE_SIGHAND` is not valid"
   214	                );
   215	            }
   216	
   217	            if clone_flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_PIDFD | CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWUSER) {
   218	                return_errno_with_message!(
   219	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   220	                    "`CLONE_THREAD` cannot be used together with `CLONE_PIDFD` or `CLONE_NEWUSER`"
   221	                );
   222	            }
   223	        }
   224	
   225	        Ok(())
   226	    }
   227	}
   228	
   229	impl From<u64> for CloneFlags {
   230	    fn from(flags: u64) -> Self {
   231	        // We use the lower 32 bits
   232	        let clone_flags = (flags & 0xffff_ffff) as u32;
   233	        CloneFlags::from_bits_truncate(clone_flags)
   234	    }
   235	}
   236	
   237	impl CloneFlags {
   238	    fn check_unsupported_flags(&self) -> Result<()> {
   239	        let supported_flags = CloneFlags::CLONE_VM
   240	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_FS
   241	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_FILES
   242	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND
   243	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_PIDFD
   244	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD
   245	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_SYSVSEM
   246	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_SETTLS
   247	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT_SETTID
   248	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_CHILD_SETTID
   249	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID
   250	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_VFORK
   251	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS
   252	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT;
   253	        let unsupported_flags = *self - supported_flags;
   254	        if !unsupported_flags.is_empty() {
   255	            warn!("contains unsupported clone flags: {:?}", unsupported_flags);
   256	        }
   257	        Ok(())
   258	    }
   259	}
   260	
   261	/// Clone a child thread or child process.
   262	///
   263	/// FIXME: currently, the child process or thread will be scheduled to run at once,
   264	/// but this may not be the expected behavior.
   265	pub fn clone_child(
   266	    ctx: &Context,
   267	    parent_context: &UserContext,
   268	    clone_args: CloneArgs,
   269	) -> Result<Tid> {
   270	    clone_args.check(ctx)?;
   271	
   272	    if clone_args.flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD) {
   273	        let child_task = clone_child_task(ctx, parent_context, clone_args)?;
   274	        let child_thread = child_task.as_thread().unwrap();
   275	        child_thread.run();
   276	
   277	        let child_tid = child_thread.as_posix_thread().unwrap().tid();
   278	        Ok(child_tid)
   279	    } else {
   280	        let child_process = clone_child_process(ctx, parent_context, clone_args)?;
   281	        if clone_args.flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_VFORK) {
   282	            child_process.status().set_vfork_child(true);
   283	        }
   284	
   285	        child_process.run();
   286	
   287	        if child_process.status().is_vfork_child() {
   288	            let cond = || (!child_process.status().is_vfork_child()).then_some(());
   289	            let current = ctx.process.as_ref();
   290	            current.children_wait_queue().wait_until(cond);
   291	        }
   292	
   293	        let child_pid = child_process.pid();
   294	        FORKS_COUNTER
   295	            .get()
   296	            .unwrap()
   297	            .add_on_cpu(CpuId::current_racy(), 1);
   298	        Ok(child_pid)
   299	    }
   300	}
   301	
   302	fn clone_child_task(
   303	    ctx: &Context,
   304	    parent_context: &UserContext,
   305	    clone_args: CloneArgs,
   306	) -> Result<Arc<Task>> {
   307	    let clone_flags = clone_args.flags;
   308	
   309	    let Context {
   310	        process,
   311	        thread_local,
   312	        posix_thread,
   313	        ..
   314	    } = ctx;
   315	
   316	    // Clone system V semaphore
   317	    clone_sysvsem(clone_flags)?;
   318	
   319	    // Clone file table
   320	    let child_file_table = clone_files(thread_local.borrow_file_table().unwrap(), clone_flags);
   321	
   322	    // Clone fs
   323	    let child_fs = clone_fs(&thread_local.borrow_fs(), clone_flags);
   324	
   325	    // Clone FPU context
   326	    let child_fpu_context = thread_local.fpu().clone_context();
   327	
   328	    // Clone namespaces
   329	    let child_user_ns = thread_local.borrow_user_ns().clone();
   330	    let child_ns_proxy = clone_ns_proxy(
   331	        thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy().unwrap(),
   332	        &child_user_ns,
   333	        clone_flags,
   334	        posix_thread,
   335	    )?;
   336	
   337	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS) {
   338	        child_fs
   339	            .resolver()
   340	            .write()
   341	            .switch_to_mnt_ns(child_ns_proxy.mnt_ns())?;
   342	    }
   343	
   344	    let child_user_ctx = Box::new(clone_user_ctx(
   345	        parent_context,
   346	        clone_args.stack,
   347	        clone_args.stack_size,
   348	        clone_args.tls,
   349	        clone_flags,
   350	    ));
   351	
   352	    // Inherit sigmask from current thread
   353	    let sig_mask = posix_thread.sig_mask().load(Ordering::Relaxed).into();
   354	
   355	    // Inherit the thread name.
   356	    let thread_name = posix_thread.thread_name().lock().as_ref().cloned();
   357	
   358	    let child_tid = allocate_posix_tid();
   359	    let child_task = {
   360	        let credentials = {
   361	            let credentials = ctx.posix_thread.credentials();
   362	            Credentials::new_from(&credentials)
   363	        };
   364	
   365	        let mut thread_builder = PosixThreadBuilder::new(child_tid, child_user_ctx, credentials)
   366	            .process(posix_thread.weak_process())
   367	            .thread_name(thread_name)
   368	            .sig_mask(sig_mask)
   369	            .file_table(child_file_table)
   370	            .fs(child_fs)
   371	            .fpu_context(child_fpu_context)
   372	            .user_ns(child_user_ns)
   373	            .ns_proxy(child_ns_proxy);
   374	
   375	        // Deal with SETTID/CLEARTID flags
   376	        clone_parent_settid(child_tid, clone_args.parent_tid, clone_flags)?;
   377	        thread_builder = clone_child_cleartid(thread_builder, clone_args.child_tid, clone_flags);
   378	        thread_builder = clone_child_settid(thread_builder, clone_args.child_tid, clone_flags);
   379	
   380	        thread_builder.build()
   381	    };
   382	
   383	    process
   384	        .tasks()
   385	        .lock()
   386	        .insert(child_task.clone())
   387	        .map_err(|_| Error::with_message(Errno::EINTR, "the process has exited"))?;
   388	
   389	    Ok(child_task)
   390	}
   391	
   392	fn clone_child_process(
   393	    ctx: &Context,
   394	    parent_context: &UserContext,
   395	    clone_args: CloneArgs,
   396	) -> Result<Arc<Process>> {
   397	    let Context {
   398	        process,
   399	        thread_local,
   400	        posix_thread,
   401	        ..
   402	    } = ctx;
   403	
   404	    let clone_flags = clone_args.flags;
   405	
   406	    // Clone the virtual memory space
   407	    let child_process_vm = {
   408	        let parent_process_vm = process.vm();
   409	        clone_vm(parent_process_vm, clone_flags)?
   410	    };
   411	
   412	    // Clone the user context
   413	    let child_user_ctx = Box::new(clone_user_ctx(
   414	        parent_context,
   415	        clone_args.stack,
   416	        clone_args.stack_size,
   417	        clone_args.tls,
   418	        clone_flags,
   419	    ));
   420	
   421	    // Clone the file table
   422	    let child_file_table = clone_files(thread_local.borrow_file_table().unwrap(), clone_flags);
   423	
   424	    // Clone the filesystem information
   425	    let child_fs = clone_fs(&thread_local.borrow_fs(), clone_flags);
   426	
   427	    // Clone signal dispositions
   428	    let child_sig_dispositions = clone_sighand(process.sig_dispositions(), clone_flags);
   429	
   430	    // Clone System V semaphore
   431	    clone_sysvsem(clone_flags)?;
   432	
   433	    // Clone FPU context
   434	    let child_fpu_context = thread_local.fpu().clone_context();
   435	
   436	    // Clone the namespaces
   437	    let child_user_ns = clone_user_ns(clone_flags, thread_local)?;
   438	    let child_ns_proxy = clone_ns_proxy(
   439	        thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy().unwrap(),
   440	        &child_user_ns,
   441	        clone_flags,
   442	        posix_thread,
   443	    )?;
   444	
   445	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS) {
   446	        child_fs
   447	            .resolver()
   448	            .write()
   449	            .switch_to_mnt_ns(child_ns_proxy.mnt_ns())?;
   450	    }
   451	
   452	    // Inherit the parent's signal mask
   453	    let child_sig_mask = posix_thread.sig_mask().load(Ordering::Relaxed).into();
   454	
   455	    // Inherit the parent's resource limits
   456	    let child_resource_limits = process.resource_limits().clone();
   457	
   458	    // Inherit the parent's nice value
   459	    let child_nice = process.nice().load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   460	
   461	    // Inherit the parent's OOM score adjustment
   462	    let child_oom_score_adj = process.oom_score_adj().load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   463	
   464	    let child_tid = allocate_posix_tid();
   465	
   466	    let child = {
   467	        let child_elf_path = process.executable_path();
   468	        let mut child_thread_builder = {
   469	            let child_thread_name = ThreadName::new_from_executable_path(&child_elf_path)?;
   470	
   471	            let credentials = {
   472	                let credentials = ctx.posix_thread.credentials();
   473	                Credentials::new_from(&credentials)
   474	            };
   475	
   476	            PosixThreadBuilder::new(child_tid, child_user_ctx, credentials)
   477	                .thread_name(Some(child_thread_name))
   478	                .sig_mask(child_sig_mask)
   479	                .file_table(child_file_table)
   480	                .fs(child_fs)
   481	                .fpu_context(child_fpu_context)
   482	                .user_ns(child_user_ns.clone())
   483	                .ns_proxy(child_ns_proxy)
   484	        };
   485	
   486	        // Deal with SETTID/CLEARTID flags
   487	        clone_parent_settid(child_tid, clone_args.parent_tid, clone_flags)?;
   488	        child_thread_builder =
   489	            clone_child_cleartid(child_thread_builder, clone_args.child_tid, clone_flags);
   490	        child_thread_builder =
   491	            clone_child_settid(child_thread_builder, clone_args.child_tid, clone_flags);
   492	
   493	        create_child_process(
   494	            child_tid,
   495	            &child_elf_path,
   496	            child_process_vm,
   497	            child_resource_limits,
   498	            child_nice,
   499	            child_oom_score_adj,
   500	            child_sig_dispositions,
   501	            child_user_ns,
   502	            child_thread_builder,
   503	        )
   504	    };
   505	
   506	    clone_pidfd(ctx, &child, clone_flags, clone_args.pidfd)?;
   507	
   508	    if let Some(sig) = clone_args.exit_signal {
   509	        child.set_exit_signal(sig);
   510	    };
   511	
   512	    // Sets parent process and group for child process.
   513	    set_parent_and_group(clone_flags, process, &child);
   514	
   515	    Ok(child)
   516	}
   517	
   518	fn clone_child_cleartid(
   519	    child_builder: PosixThreadBuilder,
   520	    child_tidptr: Vaddr,
   521	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   522	) -> PosixThreadBuilder {
   523	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID) {
   524	        child_builder.clear_child_tid(child_tidptr)
   525	    } else {
   526	        child_builder
   527	    }
   528	}
   529	
   530	fn clone_child_settid(
   531	    child_builder: PosixThreadBuilder,
   532	    child_tidptr: Vaddr,
   533	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   534	) -> PosixThreadBuilder {
   535	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_CHILD_SETTID) {
   536	        child_builder.set_child_tid(child_tidptr)
   537	    } else {
   538	        child_builder
   539	    }
   540	}
   541	
   542	fn clone_parent_settid(
   543	    child_tid: Tid,
   544	    parent_tidptr: Option<Vaddr>,
   545	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   546	) -> Result<()> {
   547	    if let Some(addr) =
   548	        parent_tidptr.filter(|_| clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT_SETTID))
   549	    {
   550	        current_userspace!().write_val(addr, &child_tid)?;
   551	    }
   552	    Ok(())
   553	}
   554	
   555	/// Clone child process vm. If CLONE_VM is set, both threads share the same root vmar.
   556	/// Otherwise, fork a new copy-on-write vmar.
   557	fn clone_vm(parent_process_vm: &ProcessVm, clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> Result<ProcessVm> {
   558	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_VM) {
   559	        Ok(parent_process_vm.clone())
   560	    } else {
   561	        ProcessVm::fork_from(parent_process_vm)
   562	    }
   563	}
   564	
   565	fn clone_user_ctx(
   566	    parent_context: &UserContext,
   567	    new_sp: u64,
   568	    stack_size: Option<NonZeroU64>,
   569	    tls: u64,
   570	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   571	) -> UserContext {
   572	    let mut child_context = parent_context.clone();
   573	    // The return value of child thread is zero
   574	    child_context.set_syscall_ret(0);
   575	
   576	    if new_sp != 0 {
   577	        // If stack size is not 0, the `new_sp` points to the BOTTOMMOST byte of stack.
   578	        if let Some(size) = stack_size {
   579	            child_context.set_stack_pointer((new_sp + size.get()) as usize)
   580	        }
   581	        // If stack size is 0, the new_sp points to the TOPMOST byte of stack.
   582	        else {
   583	            child_context.set_stack_pointer(new_sp as usize);
   584	        }
   585	    }
   586	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SETTLS) {
   587	        child_context.set_tls_pointer(tls as usize);
   588	    }
   589	
   590	    child_context
   591	}
   592	
   593	fn clone_fs(parent_fs: &Arc<ThreadFsInfo>, clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> Arc<ThreadFsInfo> {
   594	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_FS) {
   595	        parent_fs.clone()
   596	    } else {
   597	        Arc::new(parent_fs.as_ref().clone())
   598	    }
   599	}
   600	
   601	fn clone_files(parent_file_table: &RwArc<FileTable>, clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> RwArc<FileTable> {
   602	    // if CLONE_FILES is set, the child and parent shares the same file table
   603	    // Otherwise, the child will deep copy a new file table.
   604	    // FIXME: the clone may not be deep copy.
   605	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_FILES) {
   606	        parent_file_table.clone()
   607	    } else {
   608	        RwArc::new(parent_file_table.read().clone())
   609	    }
   610	}
   611	
   612	fn clone_sighand(
   613	    parent_sig_dispositions: &Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>>,
   614	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   615	) -> Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>> {
   616	    // similar to CLONE_FILES
   617	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND) {
   618	        parent_sig_dispositions.clone()
   619	    } else {
   620	        Arc::new(Mutex::new(*parent_sig_dispositions.lock()))
   621	    }
   622	}
   623	
   624	fn clone_sysvsem(clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> Result<()> {
   625	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SYSVSEM) {
   626	        warn!("CLONE_SYSVSEM is not supported now");
   627	    }
   628	    Ok(())
   629	}
   630	
   631	fn clone_pidfd(
   632	    ctx: &Context,
   633	    child: &Arc<Process>,
   634	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   635	    pidfd_addr: Option<Vaddr>,
   636	) -> Result<()> {
   637	    if !clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PIDFD) {
   638	        return Ok(());
   639	    }
   640	
   641	    let pidfd_addr = pidfd_addr.unwrap();
   642	
   643	    let fd = {
   644	        let pid_file = PidFile::new(child.clone(), false);
   645	        let file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table();
   646	        let mut file_table_locked = file_table.unwrap().write();
   647	        file_table_locked.insert(Arc::new(pid_file), FdFlags::CLOEXEC)
   648	    };
   649	
   650	    // Since `write_val` may sleep, we cannot hold the file table lock during its execution.
   651	    // FIXME: Should we remove the file from the file table if the write operation fails?
   652	    match ctx.user_space().write_val(pidfd_addr, &fd) {
   653	        Ok(()) => Ok(()),
   654	        Err(e) => {
   655	            let file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table();
   656	            let mut file_table_locked = file_table.unwrap().write();
   657	            file_table_locked.close_file(fd);
   658	            Err(e)
   659	        }
   660	    }
   661	}
   662	
   663	fn clone_user_ns(
   664	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   665	    thread_local: &ThreadLocal,
   666	) -> Result<Arc<UserNamespace>> {
   667	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWUSER) {
   668	        return_errno_with_message!(
   669	            Errno::EINVAL,
   670	            "cloning a new user namespace is not supported"
   671	        );
   672	    } else {
   673	        Ok(thread_local.borrow_user_ns().clone())
   674	    }
   675	}
   676	
   677	fn clone_ns_proxy(
   678	    parent_ns_proxy: &Arc<NsProxy>,
   679	    user_ns: &Arc<UserNamespace>,
   680	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   681	    posix_thread: &PosixThread,
   682	) -> Result<Arc<NsProxy>> {
   683	    parent_ns_proxy.new_clone(user_ns, clone_flags, posix_thread)
   684	}
   685	
   686	#[expect(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
   687	fn create_child_process(
   688	    pid: Pid,
   689	    executable_path: &str,
   690	    process_vm: ProcessVm,
   691	    resource_limits: ResourceLimits,
   692	    nice: Nice,
   693	    oom_score_adj: i16,
   694	    sig_dispositions: Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>>,
   695	    user_ns: Arc<UserNamespace>,
   696	    thread_builder: PosixThreadBuilder,
   697	) -> Arc<Process> {
   698	    let child_proc = Process::new(
   699	        pid,
   700	        executable_path.to_string(),
   701	        process_vm,
   702	        resource_limits,
   703	        nice,
   704	        oom_score_adj,
   705	        sig_dispositions,
   706	        user_ns,
   707	    );
   708	
   709	    let child_task = thread_builder.process(Arc::downgrade(&child_proc)).build();
   710	    child_proc.tasks().lock().insert(child_task).unwrap();
   711	
   712	    child_proc
   713	}
   714	
   715	fn set_parent_and_group(clone_flags: CloneFlags, parent: &Arc<Process>, child: &Arc<Process>) {
   716	    loop {
   717	        let real_parent = clone_parent(clone_flags, parent);
   718	
   719	        // Lock the parent's children before checking its status.
   720	        let mut children_mut = real_parent.children().lock();
   721	
   722	        let Some(children_mut) = children_mut.as_mut() else {
   723	            // The real parent is concurrently exiting group.
   724	            // The children have been cleared,
   725	            // so the retrial will see an up-to-date real parent.
   726	            continue;
   727	        };
   728	
   729	        // Lock order: children of process -> parent of process
   730	        child.parent().lock().set_process(&real_parent);
   731	
   732	        // Update `has_child_subreaper` for the child process to
   733	        // make sure the `has_child_subreaper` state of the child process
   734	        // will be consistent with its parent.
   735	        if real_parent.has_child_subreaper.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   736	            child.has_child_subreaper.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   737	        }
   738	
   739	        // Lock order: children of process -> process table
   740	        // -> group of process -> group inner
   741	
   742	        let mut process_table_mut = process_table::process_table_mut();
   743	
   744	        let process_group_mut = parent.process_group.lock();
   745	
   746	        let process_group = process_group_mut.upgrade().unwrap();
   747	        let mut process_group_inner = process_group.lock();
   748	
   749	        // Put the child process in the parent's process group
   750	        process_group_inner.insert_process(child.clone());
   751	        *child.process_group.lock() = Arc::downgrade(&process_group);
   752	
   753	        // Put the child process in the parent's `children` field
   754	        children_mut.insert(child.pid(), child.clone());
   755	
   756	        // Put the child process in the global table
   757	        process_table_mut.insert(child.pid(), child.clone());
   758	
   759	        return;
   760	    }
   761	}
   762	
   763	fn clone_parent(clone_flags: CloneFlags, current: &Arc<Process>) -> Cow<'_, Arc<Process>> {
   764	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT) {
   765	        // The parent process of the current process cannot be `None`, since we have checked that
   766	        // the current process is not the init process.
   767	        Cow::Owned(current.parent().lock().process().upgrade().unwrap())
   768	    } else {
   769	        Cow::Borrowed(current)
   770	    }
   771	}

===== kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs =====
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	use core::num::NonZeroU64;
     4	
     5	use ostd::arch::cpu::context::UserContext;
     6	
     7	use super::SyscallReturn;
     8	use crate::{
     9	    prelude::*,
    10	    process::{clone_child, signal::sig_num::SigNum, CloneArgs, CloneFlags},
    11	};
    12	
    13	// The order of arguments for clone differs in different architecture.
    14	// This order we use here is the order for x86_64. See https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/clone.2.html.
    15	pub fn sys_clone(
    16	    clone_flags: u64,
    17	    new_sp: u64,
    18	    parent_tidptr: Vaddr,
    19	    child_tidptr: Vaddr,
    20	    tls: u64,
    21	    ctx: &Context,
    22	    parent_context: &UserContext,
    23	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    24	    let args = CloneArgs::for_clone(clone_flags, parent_tidptr, child_tidptr, tls, new_sp)?;
    25	    debug!("flags = {:?}, child_stack_ptr = 0x{:x}, parent_tid_ptr = 0x{:x?}, child tid ptr = 0x{:x}, tls = 0x{:x}", args.flags, args.stack, args.parent_tid, args.child_tid, args.tls);
    26	    let child_pid = clone_child(ctx, parent_context, args)?;
    27	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(child_pid as _))
    28	}
    29	
    30	pub fn sys_clone3(
    31	    clong_args_addr: Vaddr,
    32	    size: usize,
    33	    ctx: &Context,
    34	    parent_context: &UserContext,
    35	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    36	    trace!(
    37	        "clone args addr = 0x{:x}, size = 0x{:x}",
    38	        clong_args_addr,
    39	        size
    40	    );
    41	    if size != size_of::<Clone3Args>() {
    42	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid size");
    43	    }
    44	
    45	    let clone_args = {
    46	        let args: Clone3Args = ctx.user_space().read_val(clong_args_addr)?;
    47	        trace!("clone3 args = {:x?}", args);
    48	        CloneArgs::try_from(args)?
    49	    };
    50	    debug!("clone args = {:x?}", clone_args);
    51	
    52	    let child_pid = clone_child(ctx, parent_context, clone_args)?;
    53	    trace!("child pid = {}", child_pid);
    54	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(child_pid as _))
    55	}
    56	
    57	#[repr(C)]
    58	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Pod)]
    59	struct Clone3Args {
    60	    /// Flags bit mask
    61	    flags: u64,
    62	    /// Where to store PID file descriptor
    63	    pidfd: u64,
    64	    /// Where to store child TID in child's memory
    65	    child_tid: u64,
    66	    ///  Where to store child TID in parent's memory
    67	    parent_tid: u64,
    68	    /// Signal to deliver to parent on child termination
    69	    exit_signal: u64,
    70	    /// Pointer to lowest byte of stack
    71	    stack: u64,
    72	    /// Size of stack
    73	    stack_size: u64,
    74	    /// Location of new TLS
    75	    tls: u64,
    76	    /// Pointer to a pid_t array
    77	    set_tid: u64,
    78	    /// Number of elements in set_tid
    79	    set_tid_size: u64,
    80	    /// File descriptor for target cgroup of child
    81	    cgroup: u64,
    82	}
    83	
    84	impl TryFrom<Clone3Args> for CloneArgs {
    85	    type Error = Error;
    86	
    87	    fn try_from(value: Clone3Args) -> Result<Self> {
    88	        // TODO: Deal with set_tid, set_tid_size, cgroup
    89	        if value.set_tid != 0 || value.set_tid_size != 0 {
    90	            warn!("set_tid is not supported");
    91	        }
    92	
    93	        if value.cgroup != 0 {
    94	            warn!("cgroup is not supported");
    95	        }
    96	
    97	        let flags = CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)
    98	            .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid clone flags"))?;
    99	        let exit_signal =
   100	            (value.exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(value.exit_signal as u8));
   101	
   102	        if flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT | CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD)
   103	            && exit_signal.is_some()
   104	        {
   105	            return_errno_with_message!(
   106	                Errno::EINVAL,
   107	                "`CLONE_PARENT` and  `CLONE_THREAD` cannot be used if the exit signal is specified"
   108	            )
   109	        }
   110	
   111	        Ok(Self {
   112	            flags,
   113	            pidfd: Some(value.pidfd as Vaddr),
   114	            child_tid: value.child_tid as _,
   115	            parent_tid: Some(value.parent_tid as _),
   116	            exit_signal,
   117	            stack: value.stack,
   118	            stack_size: NonZeroU64::new(value.stack_size),
   119	            tls: value.tls,
   120	            _set_tid: Some(value.set_tid),
   121	            _set_tid_size: Some(value.set_tid_size),
   122	            _cgroup: Some(value.cgroup),
   123	        })
   124	    }
   125	}

===== kernel/src/process/exit.rs =====
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	use core::sync::atomic::Ordering;
     4	
     5	use super::{process_table, Pid, Process};
     6	use crate::{events::IoEvents, prelude::*, process::signal::signals::kernel::KernelSignal};
     7	
     8	/// Exits the current POSIX process.
     9	///
    10	/// This is for internal use. Do NOT call this directly. When the last thread in the process exits,
    11	/// [`do_exit`] or [`do_exit_group`] will invoke this method automatically.
    12	///
    13	/// [`do_exit`]: crate::process::posix_thread::do_exit
    14	/// [`do_exit_group`]: crate::process::posix_thread::do_exit_group
    15	pub(super) fn exit_process(current_process: &Process) {
    16	    current_process.status().set_zombie();
    17	    current_process.status().set_vfork_child(false);
    18	
    19	    // Drop fields in `Process`.
    20	    current_process.lock_root_vmar().set_vmar(None);
    21	
    22	    current_process.pidfile_pollee.notify(IoEvents::IN);
    23	
    24	    send_parent_death_signal(current_process);
    25	
    26	    move_children_to_reaper_process(current_process);
    27	
    28	    send_child_death_signal(current_process);
    29	}
    30	
    31	/// Sends parent-death signals to the children.
    32	//
    33	// FIXME: According to the Linux implementation, the signal should be sent when the POSIX thread
    34	// that created the child exits, not when the whole process exits. For more details, see the
    35	// "CAVEATS" section in <https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/pr_set_pdeathsig.2const.html>.
    36	fn send_parent_death_signal(current_process: &Process) {
    37	    let current_children = current_process.children().lock();
    38	    for child in current_children.as_ref().unwrap().values() {
    39	        let Some(signum) = child.parent_death_signal() else {
    40	            continue;
    41	        };
    42	
    43	        // FIXME: Set `si_pid` in the `siginfo_t` argument.
    44	        let signal = KernelSignal::new(signum);
    45	        child.enqueue_signal(signal);
    46	    }
    47	}
    48	
    49	/// Finds a reaper process for `current_process`.
    50	///
    51	/// If there is no reaper process for `current_process`, returns `None`.
    52	fn find_reaper_process(current_process: &Process) -> Option<Arc<Process>> {
    53	    let mut parent = current_process.parent().lock().process();
    54	
    55	    while let Some(process) = parent.upgrade() {
    56	        if is_init_process(&process) {
    57	            return Some(process);
    58	        }
    59	
    60	        if !process.has_child_subreaper.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
    61	            return None;
    62	        }
    63	
    64	        let is_reaper = process.is_child_subreaper();
    65	        let is_zombie = process.status().is_zombie();
    66	        if is_reaper && !is_zombie {
    67	            return Some(process);
    68	        }
    69	
    70	        parent = process.parent().lock().process();
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    None
    74	}
    75	
    76	/// Moves the children of `current_process` to be the children of `reaper_process`.
    77	///
    78	/// If the `reaper_process` is zombie, returns `Err(())`.
    79	fn move_process_children(
    80	    current_process: &Process,
    81	    reaper_process: &Arc<Process>,
    82	) -> core::result::Result<(), ()> {
    83	    // Take the lock first to avoid the race when the `reaper_process` is exiting concurrently.
    84	    let mut reaper_process_children = reaper_process.children().lock();
    85	
    86	    let Some(reaper_process_children) = reaper_process_children.as_mut() else {
    87	        // The reaper process has exited, and it is not the init process
    88	        // (since we never clear the init process's children).
    89	        return Err(());
    90	    };
    91	
    92	    // Lock order: children of process -> parent of process
    93	    // We holds the lock of children while update the children's parents.
    94	    // This ensures when dealing with CLONE_PARENT,
    95	    // the retrial will see an up-to-date real parent.
    96	    let mut current_children = current_process.children().lock();
    97	    for child_process in current_children.as_mut().unwrap().values() {
    98	        let mut parent = child_process.parent.lock();
    99	        reaper_process_children.insert(child_process.pid(), child_process.clone());
   100	        parent.set_process(reaper_process);
   101	    }
   102	    *current_children = None;
   103	
   104	    Ok(())
   105	}
   106	
   107	/// Moves the children to a reaper process.
   108	fn move_children_to_reaper_process(current_process: &Process) {
   109	    if is_init_process(current_process) {
   110	        return;
   111	    }
   112	
   113	    while let Some(reaper_process) = find_reaper_process(current_process) {
   114	        if move_process_children(current_process, &reaper_process).is_ok() {
   115	            return;
   116	        }
   117	    }
   118	
   119	    let Some(init_process) = get_init_process() else {
   120	        return;
   121	    };
   122	
   123	    let _ = move_process_children(current_process, &init_process);
   124	}
   125	
   126	/// Sends a child-death signal to the parent.
   127	fn send_child_death_signal(current_process: &Process) {
   128	    let Some(parent) = current_process.parent().lock().process().upgrade() else {
   129	        return;
   130	    };
   131	
   132	    if let Some(signal) = current_process.exit_signal().map(KernelSignal::new) {
   133	        parent.enqueue_signal(signal);
   134	    };
   135	    parent.children_wait_queue().wake_all();
   136	}
   137	
   138	const INIT_PROCESS_PID: Pid = 1;
   139	
   140	/// Gets the init process
   141	fn get_init_process() -> Option<Arc<Process>> {
   142	    process_table::get_process(INIT_PROCESS_PID)
   143	}
   144	
   145	fn is_init_process(process: &Process) -> bool {
   146	    process.pid() == INIT_PROCESS_PID
   147	}

    37	use atomic_integer_wrapper::define_atomic_version_of_integer_like_type;
