    89	        if value.set_tid != 0 || value.set_tid_size != 0 {
    90	            warn!("set_tid is not supported");
    91	        }
    92	
    93	        if value.cgroup != 0 {
    94	            warn!("cgroup is not supported");
    95	        }
    96	
    97	        let flags = CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)
    98	            .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid clone flags"))?;
    99	        let exit_signal =
   100	            (value.exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(value.exit_signal as u8));
   101	
   102	        if flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT | CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD)
   103	            && exit_signal.is_some()
   104	        {
   105	            return_errno_with_message!(
   106	                Errno::EINVAL,
   107	                "`CLONE_PARENT` and  `CLONE_THREAD` cannot be used if the exit signal is specified"
   108	            )
   109	        }
   110	
   111	        Ok(Self {
   112	            flags,
   113	            pidfd: Some(value.pidfd as Vaddr),
   114	            child_tid: value.child_tid as _,
   115	            parent_tid: Some(value.parent_tid as _),
   116	            exit_signal,
   117	            stack: value.stack,
   118	            stack_size: NonZeroU64::new(value.stack_size),
   119	            tls: value.tls,
   120	            _set_tid: Some(value.set_tid),
   121	            _set_tid_size: Some(value.set_tid_size),
   122	            _cgroup: Some(value.cgroup),
   123	        })
   124	    }
   125	}

===== kernel/src/process/exit.rs =====
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	use core::sync::atomic::Ordering;
     4	
     5	use super::{process_table, Pid, Process};
     6	use crate::{events::IoEvents, prelude::*, process::signal::signals::kernel::KernelSignal};
     7	
     8	/// Exits the current POSIX process.
     9	///
    10	/// This is for internal use. Do NOT call this directly. When the last thread in the process exits,
    11	/// [`do_exit`] or [`do_exit_group`] will invoke this method automatically.
    12	///
    13	/// [`do_exit`]: crate::process::posix_thread::do_exit
    14	/// [`do_exit_group`]: crate::process::posix_thread::do_exit_group
    15	pub(super) fn exit_process(current_process: &Process) {
    16	    current_process.status().set_zombie();
    17	    current_process.status().set_vfork_child(false);
    18	
    19	    // Drop fields in `Process`.
    20	    current_process.lock_root_vmar().set_vmar(None);
    21	
    22	    current_process.pidfile_pollee.notify(IoEvents::IN);
    23	
    24	    send_parent_death_signal(current_process);
    25	
    26	    move_children_to_reaper_process(current_process);
    27	
    28	    send_child_death_signal(current_process);
    29	}
    30	
    31	/// Sends parent-death signals to the children.
    32	//
    33	// FIXME: According to the Linux implementation, the signal should be sent when the POSIX thread
    34	// that created the child exits, not when the whole process exits. For more details, see the
    35	// "CAVEATS" section in <https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/pr_set_pdeathsig.2const.html>.
    36	fn send_parent_death_signal(current_process: &Process) {
    37	    let current_children = current_process.children().lock();
    38	    for child in current_children.as_ref().unwrap().values() {
    39	        let Some(signum) = child.parent_death_signal() else {
    40	            continue;
    41	        };
    42	
    43	        // FIXME: Set `si_pid` in the `siginfo_t` argument.
    44	        let signal = KernelSignal::new(signum);
    45	        child.enqueue_signal(signal);
    46	    }
    47	}
    48	
    49	/// Finds a reaper process for `current_process`.
    50	///
    51	/// If there is no reaper process for `current_process`, returns `None`.
    52	fn find_reaper_process(current_process: &Process) -> Option<Arc<Process>> {
    53	    let mut parent = current_process.parent().lock().process();
    54	
    55	    while let Some(process) = parent.upgrade() {
    56	        if is_init_process(&process) {
    57	            return Some(process);
    58	        }
    59	
    60	        if !process.has_child_subreaper.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
    61	            return None;
    62	        }
    63	
    64	        let is_reaper = process.is_child_subreaper();
    65	        let is_zombie = process.status().is_zombie();
    66	        if is_reaper && !is_zombie {
    67	            return Some(process);
    68	        }
    69	
    70	        parent = process.parent().lock().process();
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    None
    74	}
    75	
    76	/// Moves the children of `current_process` to be the children of `reaper_process`.
    77	///
    78	/// If the `reaper_process` is zombie, returns `Err(())`.
    79	fn move_process_children(
    80	    current_process: &Process,
    81	    reaper_process: &Arc<Process>,
    82	) -> core::result::Result<(), ()> {
    83	    // Take the lock first to avoid the race when the `reaper_process` is exiting concurrently.
    84	    let mut reaper_process_children = reaper_process.children().lock();
    85	
    86	    let Some(reaper_process_children) = reaper_process_children.as_mut() else {
    87	        // The reaper process has exited, and it is not the init process
    88	        // (since we never clear the init process's children).
    89	        return Err(());
    90	    };
    91	
    92	    // Lock order: children of process -> parent of process
    93	    // We holds the lock of children while update the children's parents.
    94	    // This ensures when dealing with CLONE_PARENT,
    95	    // the retrial will see an up-to-date real parent.
    96	    let mut current_children = current_process.children().lock();
    97	    for child_process in current_children.as_mut().unwrap().values() {
    98	        let mut parent = child_process.parent.lock();
    99	        reaper_process_children.insert(child_process.pid(), child_process.clone());
   100	        parent.set_process(reaper_process);
   101	    }
   102	    *current_children = None;
   103	
   104	    Ok(())
   105	}
   106	
   107	/// Moves the children to a reaper process.
   108	fn move_children_to_reaper_process(current_process: &Process) {
   109	    if is_init_process(current_process) {
   110	        return;
   111	    }
   112	
   113	    while let Some(reaper_process) = find_reaper_process(current_process) {
   114	        if move_process_children(current_process, &reaper_process).is_ok() {
   115	            return;
   116	        }
   117	    }
   118	
   119	    let Some(init_process) = get_init_process() else {
   120	        return;
   121	    };
   122	
   123	    let _ = move_process_children(current_process, &init_process);
   124	}
   125	
   126	/// Sends a child-death signal to the parent.
   127	fn send_child_death_signal(current_process: &Process) {
   128	    let Some(parent) = current_process.parent().lock().process().upgrade() else {
   129	        return;
   130	    };
   131	
   132	    if let Some(signal) = current_process.exit_signal().map(KernelSignal::new) {
   133	        parent.enqueue_signal(signal);
   134	    };
   135	    parent.children_wait_queue().wake_all();
   136	}
   137	
   138	const INIT_PROCESS_PID: Pid = 1;
   139	
   140	/// Gets the init process
   141	fn get_init_process() -> Option<Arc<Process>> {
   142	    process_table::get_process(INIT_PROCESS_PID)
   143	}
   144	
   145	fn is_init_process(process: &Process) -> bool {
   146	    process.pid() == INIT_PROCESS_PID
   147	}

===== kernel/src/process/process/mod.rs =====
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	use core::{
     4	    sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, AtomicI16, AtomicU32, Ordering},
     5	    time::Duration,
     6	};
     7	
     8	use self::timer_manager::PosixTimerManager;
     9	use super::{
    10	    posix_thread::AsPosixThread,
    11	    process_table,
    12	    process_vm::{Heap, InitStackReader, ProcessVm, ProcessVmarGuard},
    13	    rlimit::ResourceLimits,
    14	    signal::{
    15	        sig_disposition::SigDispositions,
    16	        sig_num::{AtomicSigNum, SigNum},
    17	        signals::Signal,
    18	    },
    19	    status::ProcessStatus,
    20	    task_set::TaskSet,
    21	};
    22	use crate::{
    23	    prelude::*,
    24	    process::{signal::Pollee, status::StopWaitStatus, UserNamespace, WaitOptions},
    25	    sched::{AtomicNice, Nice},
    26	    thread::{AsThread, Thread},
    27	    time::clocks::ProfClock,
    28	};
    29	
    30	mod init_proc;
    31	mod job_control;
    32	mod process_group;
    33	mod session;
    34	mod terminal;
    35	mod timer_manager;
    36	
    37	use atomic_integer_wrapper::define_atomic_version_of_integer_like_type;
    38	pub use init_proc::spawn_init_process;
    39	pub use job_control::JobControl;
    40	use ostd::{sync::WaitQueue, task::Task};
    41	pub use process_group::ProcessGroup;
    42	pub use session::Session;
    43	pub use terminal::Terminal;
    44	
    45	/// Process id.
    46	pub type Pid = u32;
    47	define_atomic_version_of_integer_like_type!(Pid, {
    48	    #[derive(Debug)]
    49	    pub struct AtomicPid(AtomicU32);
    50	});
    51	/// Process group id.
    52	pub type Pgid = u32;
    53	/// Session Id.
    54	pub type Sid = u32;
    55	
    56	pub type ExitCode = u32;
    57	
    58	pub(super) fn init_on_each_cpu() {
    59	    timer_manager::init_on_each_cpu();
    60	}
    61	
    62	pub(super) fn init_in_first_process(ctx: &Context) {
    63	    // FIXME: This should be done by the userspace init process.
    64	    (crate::device::tty::system_console().clone() as Arc<dyn Terminal>)
    65	        .set_control(ctx.process.as_ref())
    66	        .unwrap();
    67	}
    68	
    69	/// Process stands for a set of threads that shares the same userspace.
    70	pub struct Process {
    71	    // Immutable Part
    72	    pid: Pid,
    73	
    74	    process_vm: ProcessVm,
    75	    /// Wait for child status changed
    76	    children_wait_queue: WaitQueue,
    77	    pub(super) pidfile_pollee: Pollee,
    78	
    79	    // Mutable Part
    80	    /// The executable path.
    81	    executable_path: RwLock<String>,
    82	    /// The threads
    83	    tasks: Mutex<TaskSet>,
    84	    /// Process status
    85	    status: ProcessStatus,
    86	    /// Parent process
    87	    pub(super) parent: ParentProcess,
    88	    /// Children processes
    89	    children: Mutex<Option<BTreeMap<Pid, Arc<Process>>>>,
    90	    /// Process group
    91	    pub(super) process_group: Mutex<Weak<ProcessGroup>>,
    92	    /// The resource usage statistics of reaped child processes.
    93	    reaped_children_stats: Mutex<ReapedChildrenStats>,
    94	    /// resource limits
    95	    resource_limits: ResourceLimits,
    96	    /// Scheduling priority nice value
    97	    /// According to POSIX.1, the nice value is a per-process attribute,
    98	    /// the threads in a process should share a nice value.
    99	    nice: AtomicNice,
   100	    /// The adjustment value of the out-of-memory (OOM) killer score.
   101	    // FIXME: Support OOM killer.
   102	    oom_score_adj: AtomicI16,
   103	
   104	    // Child reaper attribute
   105	    /// Whether the process is a child subreaper.
   106	    ///
   107	    /// A subreaper can be considered as a sort of "sub-init".
   108	    /// Instead of letting the init process to reap all orphan zombie processes,
   109	    /// a subreaper can reap orphan zombie processes among its descendants.
   110	    is_child_subreaper: AtomicBool,
   111	
   112	    /// Whether the process has a subreaper that will reap it when the
   113	    /// process becomes orphaned.
   114	    ///
   115	    /// If `has_child_subreaper` is true in a `Process`, this attribute should
   116	    /// also be true for all of its descendants.
   117	    pub(super) has_child_subreaper: AtomicBool,
   118	
   119	    // Signal
   120	    /// Sig dispositions
   121	    sig_dispositions: Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>>,
   122	    /// The signal that the process should receive when parent process exits.
   123	    parent_death_signal: AtomicSigNum,
   124	
   125	    /// The signal that should be sent to the parent when this process exits.
   126	    exit_signal: AtomicSigNum,
   127	
   128	    /// A profiling clock measures the user CPU time and kernel CPU time of the current process.
   129	    prof_clock: Arc<ProfClock>,
   130	
   131	    /// A manager that manages timer resources and utilities of the process.
   132	    timer_manager: PosixTimerManager,
   133	
   134	    // Namespaces
   135	    /// The user namespace
   136	    #[expect(dead_code)]
   137	    user_ns: Mutex<Arc<UserNamespace>>,
   138	}
   139	
   140	/// Representing a parent process by holding a weak reference to it and its PID.
   141	///
   142	/// This type caches the value of the PID so that it can be retrieved cheaply.
   143	///
   144	/// The benefit of using `ParentProcess` over `(Mutex<Weak<Process>>, AtomicPid,)` is to
   145	/// enforce the invariant that the cached PID and the weak reference are always kept in sync.
   146	pub struct ParentProcess {
   147	    process: Mutex<Weak<Process>>,
   148	    pid: AtomicPid,
   149	}
   150	
   151	impl ParentProcess {
   152	    pub fn new(process: Weak<Process>) -> Self {
   153	        let pid = match process.upgrade() {
   154	            Some(process) => process.pid(),
   155	            None => 0,
   156	        };
   157	
   158	        Self {
   159	            process: Mutex::new(process),
   160	            pid: AtomicPid::new(pid),
   161	        }
   162	    }
   163	
   164	    pub fn pid(&self) -> Pid {
   165	        self.pid.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   166	    }
   167	
   168	    pub fn lock(&self) -> ParentProcessGuard<'_> {
   169	        ParentProcessGuard {
   170	            guard: self.process.lock(),
   171	            this: self,
   172	        }
   173	    }
   174	}
   175	
   176	pub struct ParentProcessGuard<'a> {
   177	    guard: MutexGuard<'a, Weak<Process>>,
   178	    this: &'a ParentProcess,
   179	}
   180	
   181	impl ParentProcessGuard<'_> {
   182	    pub fn process(&self) -> Weak<Process> {
   183	        self.guard.clone()
   184	    }
   185	
   186	    /// Update both pid and weak ref.
   187	    pub fn set_process(&mut self, new_process: &Arc<Process>) {
   188	        self.this.pid.store(new_process.pid(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   189	        *self.guard = Arc::downgrade(new_process);
   190	    }
   191	}
   192	
   193	impl Process {
   194	    /// Returns the current process.
   195	    ///
   196	    /// It returns `None` if:
   197	    ///  - the function is called in the bootstrap context;
   198	    ///  - or if the current task is not associated with a process.
   199	    pub fn current() -> Option<Arc<Process>> {
   200	        Some(Task::current()?.as_posix_thread()?.process())
   201	    }
   202	
   203	    #[expect(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
   204	    pub(super) fn new(
   205	        pid: Pid,
   206	        executable_path: String,
   207	        process_vm: ProcessVm,
   208	
   209	        resource_limits: ResourceLimits,
   210	        nice: Nice,
   211	        oom_score_adj: i16,
   212	        sig_dispositions: Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>>,
   213	        user_ns: Arc<UserNamespace>,
   214	    ) -> Arc<Self> {
   215	        // SIGCHID does not interrupt pauser. Child process will
   216	        // resume paused parent when doing exit.
   217	        let children_wait_queue = WaitQueue::new();
   218	
   219	        let prof_clock = ProfClock::new();
   220	
   221	        Arc::new_cyclic(|process_ref: &Weak<Process>| Self {
   222	            pid,
   223	            tasks: Mutex::new(TaskSet::new()),
   224	            executable_path: RwLock::new(executable_path),
   225	            process_vm,
   226	            children_wait_queue,
   227	            pidfile_pollee: Pollee::new(),
   228	            status: ProcessStatus::default(),
   229	            parent: ParentProcess::new(Weak::new()),
   230	            children: Mutex::new(Some(BTreeMap::new())),
   231	            process_group: Mutex::new(Weak::new()),
   232	            reaped_children_stats: Mutex::new(ReapedChildrenStats::default()),
   233	            is_child_subreaper: AtomicBool::new(false),
   234	            has_child_subreaper: AtomicBool::new(false),
   235	            sig_dispositions,
   236	            parent_death_signal: AtomicSigNum::new_empty(),
   237	            exit_signal: AtomicSigNum::new_empty(),
   238	            resource_limits,
   239	            nice: AtomicNice::new(nice),
   240	            oom_score_adj: AtomicI16::new(oom_score_adj),
   241	            timer_manager: PosixTimerManager::new(&prof_clock, process_ref),
   242	            prof_clock,
   243	            user_ns: Mutex::new(user_ns),
   244	        })
   245	    }
   246	
   247	    /// Runs the process.
   248	    pub(super) fn run(&self) {
   249	        let tasks = self.tasks.lock();
   250	        // when run the process, the process should has only one thread
   251	        debug_assert!(tasks.as_slice().len() == 1);
   252	        debug_assert!(!self.status().is_zombie());
   253	        let task = tasks.main().clone();
   254	        // should not hold the lock when run thread
   255	        drop(tasks);
   256	        let thread = task.as_thread().unwrap();
   257	        thread.run();
   258	    }
   259	
   260	    // *********** Basic structures ***********
   261	
   262	    pub fn pid(&self) -> Pid {
   263	        self.pid
   264	    }
   265	
   266	    /// Gets the profiling clock of the process.
   267	    pub fn prof_clock(&self) -> &Arc<ProfClock> {
   268	        &self.prof_clock
   269	    }
   270	
   271	    /// Gets the timer resources and utilities of the process.
   272	    pub fn timer_manager(&self) -> &PosixTimerManager {
   273	        &self.timer_manager
   274	    }
   275	
   276	    pub fn tasks(&self) -> &Mutex<TaskSet> {
   277	        &self.tasks
   278	    }
   279	
   280	    pub fn executable_path(&self) -> String {
   281	        self.executable_path.read().clone()
   282	    }
   283	
   284	    pub fn set_executable_path(&self, executable_path: String) {
   285	        *self.executable_path.write() = executable_path;
   286	    }
   287	
   288	    pub fn resource_limits(&self) -> &ResourceLimits {
   289	        &self.resource_limits
   290	    }
   291	
   292	    pub fn nice(&self) -> &AtomicNice {
   293	        &self.nice
   294	    }
   295	
   296	    pub fn main_thread(&self) -> Arc<Thread> {
   297	        self.tasks.lock().main().as_thread().unwrap().clone()
   298	    }
   299	
   300	    pub fn oom_score_adj(&self) -> &AtomicI16 {
   301	        &self.oom_score_adj
   302	    }
   303	
   304	    // *********** Parent and child ***********
   305	
   306	    pub fn parent(&self) -> &ParentProcess {
   307	        &self.parent
   308	    }
   309	
   310	    pub fn is_init_process(&self) -> bool {
   311	        self.parent.lock().process().upgrade().is_none()
   312	    }
   313	
   314	    pub(super) fn children(&self) -> &Mutex<Option<BTreeMap<Pid, Arc<Process>>>> {
   315	        &self.children
   316	    }
   317	
   318	    pub fn children_wait_queue(&self) -> &WaitQueue {
   319	        &self.children_wait_queue
   320	    }
   321	
   322	    pub fn reaped_children_stats(&self) -> &Mutex<ReapedChildrenStats> {
   323	        &self.reaped_children_stats
   324	    }
   325	
   326	    // *********** Process group & Session ***********
   327	
   328	    /// Returns the process group ID of the process.
   329	    //
   330	    // FIXME: If we call this method on a non-current process without holding the process table
   331	    // lock, it may return zero if the process is reaped at the same time.
   332	    pub fn pgid(&self) -> Pgid {
   333	        self.process_group
   334	            .lock()
   335	            .upgrade()
   336	            .map_or(0, |group| group.pgid())
   337	    }
   338	
   339	    /// Returns the session ID of the process.
   340	    //
   341	    // FIXME: If we call this method on a non-current process without holding the process table
   342	    // lock, it may return zero if the process is reaped at the same time.
   343	    pub fn sid(&self) -> Sid {
   344	        self.process_group
   345	            .lock()
   346	            .upgrade()
   347	            .and_then(|group| group.session())
   348	            .map_or(0, |session| session.sid())
   349	    }
   350	
   351	    /// Returns the controlling terminal of the process, if any.
   352	    pub fn terminal(&self) -> Option<Arc<dyn Terminal>> {
   353	        self.process_group
   354	            .lock()
   355	            .upgrade()
   356	            .and_then(|group| group.session())
   357	            .and_then(|session| session.lock().terminal().cloned())
   358	    }
   359	
   360	    /// Moves the process to the new session.
   361	    ///
   362	    /// This method will create a new process group in a new session, move the process to the new
   363	    /// session, and return the session ID (which is equal to the process ID and the process group
   364	    /// ID).
   365	    ///
   366	    /// # Errors
   367	    ///
   368	    /// This method will return `EPERM` if an existing process group has the same identifier as the
   369	    /// process ID. This means that the process is or was a process group leader and that the
   370	    /// process group is still alive.
   371	    pub fn to_new_session(self: &Arc<Self>) -> Result<Sid> {
   372	        // Lock order: session table -> group table -> group of process
   373	        // -> group inner -> session inner
   374	        let mut session_table_mut = process_table::session_table_mut();
   375	        let mut group_table_mut = process_table::group_table_mut();
   376	
   377	        if session_table_mut.contains_key(&self.pid) {
   378	            // FIXME: According to the Linux implementation, this check should be removed, so we'll
   379	            // return `EPERM` due to hitting the following check. However, we need to work around a
   380	            // gVisor bug. The upstream gVisor has fixed the issue in:
   381	            // <https://github.com/google/gvisor/commit/582f7bf6c0ccccaeb1215a232709df38d5d409f7>.
   382	            return Ok(self.pid);
   383	        }
   384	        if group_table_mut.contains_key(&self.pid) {
   385	            return_errno_with_message!(
   386	                Errno::EPERM,
   387	                "a process group leader cannot be moved to a new session"
   388	            );
   389	        }
   390	
   391	        let mut process_group_mut = self.process_group.lock();
   392	
   393	        self.clear_old_group_and_session(
   394	            &mut process_group_mut,
   395	            &mut session_table_mut,
   396	            &mut group_table_mut,
   397	        );
   398	
   399	        Ok(self.set_new_session(
   400	            &mut process_group_mut,
   401	            &mut session_table_mut,
   402	            &mut group_table_mut,
   403	        ))
   404	    }
   405	
   406	    pub(super) fn clear_old_group_and_session(
   407	        &self,
   408	        process_group_mut: &mut MutexGuard<Weak<ProcessGroup>>,
   409	        session_table_mut: &mut MutexGuard<BTreeMap<Sid, Arc<Session>>>,
   410	        group_table_mut: &mut MutexGuard<BTreeMap<Pgid, Arc<ProcessGroup>>>,
   411	    ) {
   412	        let process_group = process_group_mut.upgrade().unwrap();
   413	        let mut process_group_inner = process_group.lock();
   414	        let session = process_group.session().unwrap();
   415	        let mut session_inner = session.lock();
   416	
   417	        // Remove the process from the process group.
   418	        process_group_inner.remove_process(&self.pid);
   419	        if process_group_inner.is_empty() {
   420	            group_table_mut.remove(&process_group.pgid());
   421	
   422	            // Remove the process group from the session.
   423	            session_inner.remove_process_group(&process_group.pgid());
   424	            if session_inner.is_empty() {
   425	                session_table_mut.remove(&session.sid());
   426	            }
   427	        }
   428	
   429	        **process_group_mut = Weak::new();
   430	    }
   431	
   432	    fn set_new_session(
   433	        self: &Arc<Self>,
   434	        process_group_mut: &mut MutexGuard<Weak<ProcessGroup>>,
   435	        session_table_mut: &mut MutexGuard<BTreeMap<Sid, Arc<Session>>>,
   436	        group_table_mut: &mut MutexGuard<BTreeMap<Pgid, Arc<ProcessGroup>>>,
   437	    ) -> Sid {
   438	        let (session, process_group) = Session::new_pair(self.clone());
   439	        let sid = session.sid();
   440	
   441	        **process_group_mut = Arc::downgrade(&process_group);
   442	
   443	        // Insert the new session and the new process group to the global table.
   444	        session_table_mut.insert(session.sid(), session);
   445	        group_table_mut.insert(process_group.pgid(), process_group);
   446	
   447	        sid
   448	    }
   449	
   450	    /// Moves the process itself or its child process to another process group.
   451	    ///
   452	    /// The process to be moved is specified with the process ID `pid`; `self` is used only for
   453	    /// permission checking purposes (see the Errors section below), which is typically
   454	    /// `current!()` when implementing system calls.
   455	    ///
   456	    /// If `pgid` is equal to the process ID, a new process group with the given PGID will be
   457	    /// created (if it does not already exist). Then, the process will be moved to the process
   458	    /// group with the given PGID, if the process group exists and belongs to the same session as
   459	    /// the given process.
   460	    ///
   461	    /// # Errors
   462	    ///
   463	    /// This method will return `ESRCH` in following cases:
   464	    ///  * The process specified by `pid` does not exist;
   465	    ///  * The process specified by `pid` is neither `self` or a child process of `self`.
   466	    ///
   467	    /// This method will return `EPERM` in following cases:
   468	    ///  * The process is not in the same session as `self`;
   469	    ///  * The process is a session leader, but the given PGID is not the process's PID/PGID;
   470	    ///  * The process group already exists, but the group does not belong to the same session;
   471	    ///  * The process group does not exist, but `pgid` is not equal to the process ID.
   472	    pub fn move_process_to_group(&self, pid: Pid, pgid: Pgid) -> Result<()> {
   473	        // Lock order: group table -> process table -> group of process
   474	        // -> group inner -> session inner
   475	        let group_table_mut = process_table::group_table_mut();
   476	        let process_table_mut = process_table::process_table_mut();
   477	
   478	        let process = process_table_mut.get(pid).ok_or(Error::with_message(
   479	            Errno::ESRCH,
   480	            "the process to set the PGID does not exist",
   481	        ))?;
   482	
   483	        let current_session = if self.pid == process.pid() {
   484	            // There is no need to check if the session is the same in this case.
   485	            None
   486	        } else if self.pid == process.parent().pid() {
   487	            // FIXME: If the child process has called `execve`, we should fail with `EACCESS`.
   488	
   489	            // Immediately release the `self.process_group` lock to avoid deadlocks. Race
   490	            // conditions don't matter because this is used for comparison purposes only.
   491	            Some(
   492	                self.process_group
   493	                    .lock()
   494	                    .upgrade()
   495	                    .unwrap()
   496	                    .session()
   497	                    .unwrap(),
   498	            )
   499	        } else {
   500	            return_errno_with_message!(
   501	                Errno::ESRCH,
   502	                "the process to set the PGID is neither the current process nor its child process"
   503	            );
   504	        };
   505	
   506	        if let Some(new_process_group) = group_table_mut.get(&pgid).cloned() {
   507	            process.to_existing_group(current_session, group_table_mut, new_process_group)
   508	        } else if pgid == process.pid() {
   509	            process.to_new_group(current_session, group_table_mut)
   510	        } else {
   511	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "the new process group does not exist");
   512	        }
   513	    }
   514	
   515	    /// Moves the process to an existing group.
   516	    fn to_existing_group(
   517	        self: &Arc<Self>,
   518	        current_session: Option<Arc<Session>>,
   519	        mut group_table_mut: MutexGuard<BTreeMap<Pgid, Arc<ProcessGroup>>>,
   520	        new_process_group: Arc<ProcessGroup>,
   521	    ) -> Result<()> {
   522	        let mut process_group_mut = self.process_group.lock();
   523	        let process_group = process_group_mut.upgrade().unwrap();
   524	
   525	        let session = process_group.session().unwrap();
   526	        if session.sid() == self.pid {
   527	            return_errno_with_message!(
   528	                Errno::EPERM,
   529	                "a session leader cannot be moved to a new process group"
   530	            );
   531	        }
   532	        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&session, &new_process_group.session().unwrap()) {
   533	            return_errno_with_message!(
   534	                Errno::EPERM,
   535	                "the new process group does not belong to the same session"
   536	            );
   537	        }
   538	        if current_session.is_some_and(|current| !Arc::ptr_eq(&current, &session)) {
   539	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "the process belongs to a different session");
   540	        }
   541	
   542	        // Lock order: group with a smaller PGID -> group with a larger PGID
   543	        let (mut process_group_inner, mut new_group_inner) =
   544	            match process_group.pgid().cmp(&new_process_group.pgid()) {
   545	                core::cmp::Ordering::Less => {
   546	                    let process_group_inner = process_group.lock();
   547	                    let new_group_inner = new_process_group.lock();
   548	                    (process_group_inner, new_group_inner)
   549	                }
   550	                core::cmp::Ordering::Greater => {
   551	                    let new_group_inner = new_process_group.lock();
   552	                    let process_group_inner = process_group.lock();
   553	                    (process_group_inner, new_group_inner)
   554	                }
   555	                core::cmp::Ordering::Equal => return Ok(()),
   556	            };
   557	        let mut session_inner = session.lock();
   558	
   559	        // Remove the process from the old process group
   560	        process_group_inner.remove_process(&self.pid);
   561	        if process_group_inner.is_empty() {
   562	            group_table_mut.remove(&process_group.pgid());
   563	            session_inner.remove_process_group(&process_group.pgid());
   564	        }
   565	
   566	        // Insert the process to the new process group
   567	        new_group_inner.insert_process(self.clone());
   568	        *process_group_mut = Arc::downgrade(&new_process_group);
   569	
   570	        Ok(())
   571	    }
   572	
   573	    /// Creates a new process group and moves the process to the group.
   574	    fn to_new_group(
   575	        self: &Arc<Self>,
   576	        current_session: Option<Arc<Session>>,
   577	        mut group_table_mut: MutexGuard<BTreeMap<Pgid, Arc<ProcessGroup>>>,
   578	    ) -> Result<()> {
   579	        let mut process_group_mut = self.process_group.lock();
   580	
   581	        let process_group = process_group_mut.upgrade().unwrap();
   582	        let session = process_group.session().unwrap();
   583	
   584	        if current_session.is_some_and(|current| !Arc::ptr_eq(&current, &session)) {
   585	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "the process belongs to a different session");
   586	        }
   587	        if process_group.pgid() == self.pid {
   588	            // We'll hit this if the process is a session leader. There is no need to check below.
   589	            return Ok(());
   590	        }
   591	
   592	        let mut process_group_inner = process_group.lock();
   593	        let mut session_inner = session.lock();
   594	
   595	        // Remove the process from the old process group
   596	        process_group_inner.remove_process(&self.pid);
   597	        if process_group_inner.is_empty() {
   598	            group_table_mut.remove(&process_group.pgid());
   599	            session_inner.remove_process_group(&process_group.pgid());
   600	        }
   601	
   602	        // Create a new process group and insert the process to it
   603	        let new_process_group = ProcessGroup::new(self.clone(), Arc::downgrade(&session));
   604	        *process_group_mut = Arc::downgrade(&new_process_group);
   605	        group_table_mut.insert(new_process_group.pgid(), new_process_group.clone());
   606	        session_inner.insert_process_group(new_process_group);
   607	
   608	        Ok(())
   609	    }
   610	
   611	    // ************** Virtual Memory *************
   612	
   613	    pub fn vm(&self) -> &ProcessVm {
   614	        &self.process_vm
   615	    }
   616	
   617	    pub fn lock_root_vmar(&self) -> ProcessVmarGuard {
   618	        self.process_vm.lock_root_vmar()
   619	    }
   620	
   621	    pub fn heap(&self) -> &Heap {
   622	        self.process_vm.heap()
   623	    }
   624	
   625	    pub fn init_stack_reader(&self) -> InitStackReader {
   626	        self.process_vm.init_stack_reader()
   627	    }
   628	
   629	    // ****************** Signal ******************
   630	
   631	    pub fn sig_dispositions(&self) -> &Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>> {
   632	        &self.sig_dispositions
   633	    }
   634	
   635	    /// Enqueues a process-directed signal.
   636	    ///
   637	    /// This method should only be used for enqueue kernel signals and fault signals.
   638	    ///
   639	    /// The signal may be delivered to any one of the threads that does not currently have the
   640	    /// signal blocked. If more than one of the threads have the signal unblocked, then this method
   641	    /// chooses an arbitrary thread to which to deliver the signal.
   642	    //
   643	    // TODO: Restrict this method with the access control tool.
   644	    pub fn enqueue_signal(&self, signal: impl Signal + Clone + 'static) {
   645	        if self.status.is_zombie() {
   646	            return;
   647	        }
   648	
   649	        let sig_dispositions = self.sig_dispositions.lock();
   650	
   651	        // Drop the signal if it's ignored. See explanation at `enqueue_signal_locked`.
   652	        let signum = signal.num();
   653	        if sig_dispositions.get(signum).will_ignore(signum) {
   654	            return;
   655	        }
   656	
   657	        let threads = self.tasks.lock();
   658	
   659	        // Enqueue the signal to the first thread that does not block the signal.
   660	        for thread in threads.as_slice() {
   661	            let posix_thread = thread.as_posix_thread().unwrap();
   662	            if !posix_thread.has_signal_blocked(signal.num()) {
   663	                posix_thread.enqueue_signal_locked(Box::new(signal), sig_dispositions);
   664	                return;
   665	            }
   666	        }
   667	
   668	        // If all threads block the signal, enqueue the signal to the main thread.
   669	        let thread = threads.main();
   670	        let posix_thread = thread.as_posix_thread().unwrap();
   671	        posix_thread.enqueue_signal_locked(Box::new(signal), sig_dispositions);
   672	    }
   673	
   674	    /// Clears the parent death signal.
   675	    pub fn clear_parent_death_signal(&self) {
   676	        self.parent_death_signal.clear();
   677	    }
   678	
   679	    /// Sets the parent death signal as `signum`.
   680	    pub fn set_parent_death_signal(&self, sig_num: SigNum) {
   681	        self.parent_death_signal.set(sig_num);
   682	    }
   683	
   684	    /// Returns the parent death signal.
   685	    ///
   686	    /// The parent death signal is the signal will be sent to child processes
   687	    /// when the process exits.
   688	    pub fn parent_death_signal(&self) -> Option<SigNum> {
   689	        self.parent_death_signal.as_sig_num()
   690	    }
   691	
   692	    pub fn set_exit_signal(&self, sig_num: SigNum) {
   693	        self.exit_signal.set(sig_num);
   694	    }
   695	
   696	    pub fn exit_signal(&self) -> Option<SigNum> {
   697	        self.exit_signal.as_sig_num()
   698	    }
   699	
   700	    // ******************* Status ********************
   701	
   702	    /// Returns a reference to the process status.
   703	    pub fn status(&self) -> &ProcessStatus {
   704	        &self.status
   705	    }
   706	
   707	    /// Stops the process.
   708	    //
   709	    // FIXME: `ptrace` is another reason that can cause a process to stop.
   710	    // Consider extending the method signature to support `ptrace` if necessary.
   711	    pub fn stop(&self, sig_num: SigNum) {
   712	        if self.status.stop_status().stop(sig_num) {
   713	            self.wake_up_parent();
   714	        }
   715	    }
   716	
   717	    /// Resumes the stopped process.
   718	    pub fn resume(&self) {
   719	        if self.status.stop_status().resume() {
   720	            self.wake_up_parent();
   721	
   722	            // Note that the resume function is called by the thread which deals with SIGCONT,
   723	            // since SIGCONT is handled by any thread in this process, we need to wake
   724	            // up other stopped threads in the same process.
   725	            for task in self.tasks.lock().as_slice() {
   726	                let posix_thread = task.as_posix_thread().unwrap();
   727	                posix_thread.wake_signalled_waker();
   728	            }
   729	        }
   730	    }
   731	
   732	    /// Returns whether the process is stopped.
   733	    pub fn is_stopped(&self) -> bool {
   734	        self.status.stop_status().is_stopped()
   735	    }
   736	
   737	    /// Gets and clears the stop status changes for the `wait` syscall.
   738	    pub(super) fn wait_stopped_or_continued(&self, options: WaitOptions) -> Option<StopWaitStatus> {
   739	        self.status.stop_status().wait(options)
   740	    }
   741	
   742	    fn wake_up_parent(&self) {
   743	        let parent_guard = self.parent.lock();
   744	        let parent = parent_guard.process().upgrade().unwrap();
   745	        parent.children_wait_queue.wake_all();
   746	    }
   747	
   748	    // ******************* Subreaper ********************
   749	
   750	    /// Sets the child subreaper attribute of the current process.
   751	    pub fn set_child_subreaper(&self) {
   752	        self.is_child_subreaper.store(true, Ordering::Release);
   753	        let has_child_subreaper = self.has_child_subreaper.fetch_or(true, Ordering::AcqRel);
   754	        if !has_child_subreaper {
   755	            self.propagate_has_child_subreaper();
   756	        }
   757	    }
   758	
   759	    /// Unsets the child subreaper attribute of the current process.
   760	    pub fn unset_child_subreaper(&self) {
   761	        self.is_child_subreaper.store(false, Ordering::Release);
   762	    }
   763	
   764	    /// Returns whether this process is a child subreaper.
   765	    pub fn is_child_subreaper(&self) -> bool {
   766	        self.is_child_subreaper.load(Ordering::Acquire)
   767	    }
   768	
   769	    /// Sets all descendants of the current process as having child subreaper.
   770	    fn propagate_has_child_subreaper(&self) {
   771	        let mut process_queue = VecDeque::new();
   772	        let children = self.children().lock();
   773	        let Some(children_ref) = children.as_ref() else {
   774	            // The current process is exiting group at the same time.
   775	            return;
   776	        };
   777	        for child_process in children_ref.values() {
   778	            if !child_process.has_child_subreaper.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
   779	                process_queue.push_back(child_process.clone());
   780	            }
   781	        }
   782	
   783	        while let Some(process) = process_queue.pop_front() {
   784	            process.has_child_subreaper.store(true, Ordering::Release);
   785	            let children = process.children().lock();
   786	            let Some(children_ref) = children.as_ref() else {
   787	                // The process is exiting group at the same time.
   788	                continue;
   789	            };
   790	            for child_process in children_ref.values() {
   791	                if !child_process.has_child_subreaper.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
   792	                    process_queue.push_back(child_process.clone());
   793	                }
   794	            }
   795	        }
   796	    }
   797	}
   798	
   799	/// Enqueues a process-directed kernel signal asynchronously.
   800	///
   801	/// This is the asynchronous version of [`Process::enqueue_signal`]. By asynchronous, this method
   802	/// submits a work item and returns, so this method doesn't sleep and can be used in atomic mode.
   803	pub fn enqueue_signal_async(process: Weak<Process>, signum: SigNum) {
   804	    use super::signal::signals::kernel::KernelSignal;
   805	    use crate::thread::work_queue;
   806	
   807	    work_queue::submit_work_func(
   808	        move || {
   809	            if let Some(process) = process.upgrade() {
   810	                process.enqueue_signal(KernelSignal::new(signum));
   811	            }
   812	        },
   813	        work_queue::WorkPriority::High,
   814	    );
   815	}
   816	
   817	/// Broadcasts a process-directed kernel signal asynchronously.
   818	///
   819	/// This is the asynchronous version of [`ProcessGroup::broadcast_signal`]. By asynchronous, this
   820	/// method submits a work item and returns, so this method doesn't sleep and can be used in atomic
   821	/// mode.
   822	pub fn broadcast_signal_async(process_group: Weak<ProcessGroup>, signum: SigNum) {
   823	    use super::signal::signals::kernel::KernelSignal;
   824	    use crate::thread::work_queue;
   825	
   826	    work_queue::submit_work_func(
   827	        move || {
   828	            if let Some(process_group) = process_group.upgrade() {
   829	                process_group.broadcast_signal(KernelSignal::new(signum));
   830	            }
   831	        },
   832	        work_queue::WorkPriority::High,
   833	    );
   834	}
   835	
   836	/// The resource usage statistics of child processes that have terminated and
   837	/// been reaped by the parent.
   838	///
   839	/// These statistics include the resources consumed by grandchildren and more
   840	/// distant descendants, if all intermediate child processes have waited on
   841	/// their own terminated children.
   842	#[derive(Default)]
   843	pub struct ReapedChildrenStats {
   844	    user_time: Duration,
   845	    kernel_time: Duration,
   846	}
   847	
   848	impl ReapedChildrenStats {
   849	    pub fn add(&mut self, utime: Duration, stime: Duration) {
   850	        self.user_time += utime;
   851	        self.kernel_time += stime;
   852	    }
   853	
   854	    pub fn get(&self) -> (Duration, Duration) {
   855	        (self.user_time, self.kernel_time)
   856	    }
   857	}

===== kernel/src/thread/task.rs =====
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	use ostd::{
     4	    arch::cpu::context::UserContext,
     5	    sync::Waiter,
     6	    task::{Task, TaskOptions},
     7	    user::{ReturnReason, UserContextApi, UserMode},
     8	};
     9	
    10	use super::{oops, Thread};
    11	use crate::{
    12	    cpu::LinuxAbi,
    13	    current_userspace,
    14	    prelude::*,
    15	    process::{
    16	        posix_thread::{AsPosixThread, AsThreadLocal, ThreadLocal},
    17	        signal::handle_pending_signal,
    18	    },
    19	    syscall::handle_syscall,
    20	    thread::{exception::handle_exception, AsThread},
    21	    vm::vmar::is_userspace_vaddr,
    22	};
    23	
    24	/// create new task with userspace and parent process
    25	pub fn create_new_user_task(
    26	    user_ctx: Box<UserContext>,
    27	    thread_ref: Arc<Thread>,
    28	    thread_local: ThreadLocal,
    29	    is_init_process: bool,
    30	) -> Task {
    31	    let user_task_entry = move |user_ctx: UserContext| {
    32	        let current_task = Task::current().unwrap();
    33	        let current_thread = current_task.as_thread().unwrap();
    34	        let current_posix_thread = current_thread.as_posix_thread().unwrap();
    35	        let current_thread_local = current_task.as_thread_local().unwrap();
    36	        let current_process = current_posix_thread.process();
    37	        let (stop_waiter, _) = Waiter::new_pair();
    38	
    39	        let mut user_mode = UserMode::new(user_ctx);
    40	        user_mode.context_mut().activate_tls_pointer();
    41	        debug!(
    42	            "[Task entry] rip = 0x{:x}",
    43	            user_mode.context().instruction_pointer()
    44	        );
    45	        debug!(
    46	            "[Task entry] rsp = 0x{:x}",
    47	            user_mode.context().stack_pointer()
    48	        );
    49	        debug!(
    50	            "[Task entry] rax = 0x{:x}",
    51	            user_mode.context().syscall_ret()
    52	        );
    53	
    54	        // The `clone` syscall may require child process to write the thread pid to the specified address.
    55	        // Make sure the store operation completes before the clone call returns control to user space
    56	        // in the child process.
    57	        let child_tid_ptr = current_thread_local.set_child_tid().get();
    58	        if is_userspace_vaddr(child_tid_ptr) {
    59	            current_userspace!()
    60	                .write_val(child_tid_ptr, &current_posix_thread.tid())
    61	                .unwrap();
    62	        }
    63	
    64	        let has_kernel_event_fn = || current_posix_thread.has_pending();
    65	
    66	        let ctx = Context {
    67	            process: current_process,
    68	            thread_local: current_thread_local,
    69	            posix_thread: current_posix_thread,
    70	            thread: current_thread.as_ref(),
    71	            task: &current_task,
    72	        };
    73	
    74	        if is_init_process {
    75	            crate::init_in_first_process(&ctx);
    76	        }
    77	
    78	        while !current_thread.is_exited() {
    79	            // Execute the user code
    80	            ctx.thread_local.fpu().activate();
    81	            let return_reason = user_mode.execute(has_kernel_event_fn);
    82	            ctx.thread_local.fpu().deactivate();
    83	
    84	            // Handle user events
    85	            let user_ctx = user_mode.context_mut();
    86	            let mut pre_syscall_ret = None;
    87	            match return_reason {
    88	                ReturnReason::UserException => {
    89	                    let exception = user_ctx.take_exception().unwrap();
    90	                    handle_exception(&ctx, user_ctx, exception)
    91	                }
    92	                ReturnReason::UserSyscall => {
    93	                    pre_syscall_ret = Some(user_ctx.syscall_ret());
    94	                    handle_syscall(&ctx, user_ctx);
    95	                }
    96	                ReturnReason::KernelEvent => {}
    97	            };
    98	
    99	            // Exit if the thread terminates
   100	            if current_thread.is_exited() {
   101	                break;
   102	            }
   103	
   104	            // Handle signals
   105	            handle_pending_signal(user_ctx, &ctx, pre_syscall_ret);
   106	
   107	            // Handle signals while the thread is stopped
   108	            // FIXME: Currently, we handle all signals when the process is stopped.
   109	            // However, when the process is stopped, at least signals with user-provided handlers
   110	            // should not be handled; these signals should only be handled when the process is continued.
   111	            // Certain signals, such as SIGKILL, should be handled even if the process is stopped.
   112	            // We need to further investigate Linux behavior regarding which signals should be handled
   113	            // when the thread is stopped.
   114	            while !current_thread.is_exited() && ctx.process.is_stopped() {
   115	                let _ = stop_waiter.pause_until(|| (!ctx.process.is_stopped()).then_some(()));
   116	                handle_pending_signal(user_ctx, &ctx, None);
   117	            }
   118	        }
   119	    };
   120	
   121	    let user_task_func = move || user_task_entry(*user_ctx);
   122	
   123	    TaskOptions::new(move || {
   124	        // TODO: If a kernel "oops" is caught, we should kill the entire
   125	        // process rather than just ending the thread.
   126	        let _ = oops::catch_panics_as_oops(user_task_func);
   127	    })
   128	    .data(thread_ref)
   129	    .local_data(thread_local)
   130	    .build()
   131	    .expect("spawn task failed")
   132	}

---
name: aster-code-review
description: Review code against Asterinas's persona-keyed coding guidelines and write a Markdown review file. Use when asked to review a Git change (diff mode) or a set of target files (files mode) for defects, or inside a write-test-review loop.

# aster-code-review

Review code against Asterinas's coding guidelines
— the persona-keyed pages under `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/`
— and write one Markdown review file.
The review is **recall-first**: miss as few real defects as possible.
It makes both objective calls (undeniable bugs) and subjective calls (guideline violations, each grounded in a cited rule).

There are two review modes, both **anchored at the current checkout (HEAD)**:

- **`diff <base>`** — a Git change:
  the **commit series** the branch adds over its merge-base with `<base>`
  — each commit's message *and* its diff,
  so the review covers per-commit intent and commit hygiene, not just the net change.
  (Commit first; uncommitted edits are not reviewed in diff mode.)
- **`files <path[:lines] ...>`**
  — target code: the working-tree contents of the named files,
  optionally narrowed to line ranges.

To review at a specific commit, check it out first (it becomes HEAD).

This skill is agent-agnostic (Claude Code and Codex).
Only step 3's spawn primitive differs between agents;
everything else is identical.

## Interface

However the skill is triggered,
it receives ONE raw argument string that begins with a mode word:

```
diff   <base>              <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
files  <path[:lines] ...>  <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
```

- `<base>` (diff) — **required**,
  any ref or SHA;
  reviews the commit series `merge-base(<base>, HEAD)..HEAD` (each commit's message + diff).
- `<path[:lines] ...>` (files) — **required**, one or more targets in the working tree.
  A target is a path, optionally `path:N-M` or `path:N-M,K-L` (1-based, inclusive);
  repeat a path to add ranges.
  Wrap a path in double quotes to allow spaces or colons (`"a: b.rs":10-20`).
- `<output>` — **required, last positional** (`cp src... dest` style);
  refuse to overwrite unless `--overwrite`.
- `--overwrite` — replace the output file if it already exists.
- `--per-persona-context` — `yes`: fan out one isolated agent per persona (best recall).
  `no`: one combined agent reviews all personas (cheaper, lower recall).
  `auto` (default) currently resolves to `yes`;
  it will become a benchmark-driven heuristic.
  Controls step 3 only.

**Do not pre-split the argument string yourself.**
Pass it verbatim, as a single quoted argument,
to `resolve_target.sh` (step 1)
— the script self-tokenizes (whitespace-split except inside double quotes),
which keeps the parse deterministic and identical across agents.

## Pipeline

Run these steps in order.
Steps 1 and 5 are deterministic scripts;
steps 6–8 use the model.

The helper scripts live in the skill's own `scripts/` directory,
at `.agents/skills/aster-code-review/scripts/` inside the repository under review.
**Invoke every script by its absolute path**
— the working directory is the code under review (the repo root), *not* the skill directory,
so a bare `scripts/…` is not found (notably on Codex, which runs commands from the repo root; do not `cd` into the skill dir either — `resolve_target.sh` needs the repo as its cwd).
Shells do not persist between commands,
so **set `SKILL` at the start of each command that uses it** (or inline the `$(git rev-parse …)`):

```sh
SKILL="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.agents/skills/aster-code-review"; "$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'
```

The steps below write `$SKILL/scripts/…` as shorthand for that absolute path.

1. **Resolve the target.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'` prints the canonical review input
   — the commit series (each commit's message + diff) in `diff` mode,
   annotated file excerpts in `files` mode;
   save it to a temp file.
   Run it again with `--meta` first (`"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" --meta '<raw args>'`) to get
   `mode=`, `base=`/`files=`, `head=`, `branch=`, `output=`, `overwrite=`, `per_persona_context=`;
   add `date=` (today) and an optional `title=` to make the meta file.
   Pass the raw argument string as a single quoted argument
   so the script's tokenizer sees it intact.
2. **Activate personas.**
   Pick which of the five personas run,
   from the reviewed paths — changed paths in `diff` mode,
   named paths in `files` mode (see *Activation*).
3. **Fan out.**
   Spawn the persona passes (see *Spawning*).
   With `per_persona_context` = `yes` or `auto` (the default),
   run **one isolated PASS per activated persona** — best recall.
   With `no`, run **one combined PASS** over all activated personas in a single context
   — cheaper, lower recall.
   Build each pass's prompt deterministically with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
   — one persona for fan-out, all activated personas for combined
   — and spawn the sub-agent with **that exact text**.
   Each pass returns a JSON array of comments;
   file each under its persona's `<fragdir>/<persona>.json` (the comment's `persona` field says which),
   so step 5 is unchanged.
4. **Collect** the per-persona JSON fragments.
5. **Assemble.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/assemble_review.sh" [--overwrite] <meta> <fragdir> <output>` performs the deterministic merge (group by persona in fixed order, sort by file→line, drop exact duplicates *within a persona*, write frontmatter, leave a `<!-- SUMMARY -->` placeholder).
   Pass `--overwrite` only if the user gave it;
   otherwise the script refuses to clobber an existing `<output>`.
6. **Verify** (see *Verification*).
7. **Consolidate** (see *Consolidation*).
8. **Summary.**
   Replace `<!-- SUMMARY -->` with a constructive summary over the final comments:
   what the code does well, the top issues ranked by severity,
   any structural recommendations.

## Activation

A persona runs unless the reviewed paths *provably* contain nothing in its remit (reviewed paths = changed paths in `diff` mode, named paths in `files` mode):

- **maintainability, development, security** — any reviewed code.
- **hardware** — a reviewed path is assembly (`*.S`, `*.asm`),
  an architecture directory (`.../arch/...`), or contains `asm!` / `global_asm!`.
- **documentation** — a reviewed path is `book/`, any `*.md`,
  syscall-coverage files (`*.scml`), or a user-facing API surface (a syscall or kernel parameter).

Activation is path-based and deterministic;
do not use a model to triage (a wrongly-skipped persona is a silent recall hole).

## Pass contract

The shared reviewer contract
— the review methodology and the JSON comment schema every pass must follow
— lives in [`scripts/pass_contract.md`](scripts/pass_contract.md),
inlined as the **stable head** of every pass prompt by `build_pass_prompt.sh` (step 3).
Do not paraphrase it into the prompt yourself:
retyping it breaks the byte-identical prefix the prompt cache relies on (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).
A pass reads only the persona block(s) it is given (selective exposure),
reviews the **REVIEW INPUT** at the foot of the prompt,
and returns a JSON array of comments per that schema.

## Spawning a pass

Build every pass prompt with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
and spawn the sub-agent with its **exact** output
— only a script keeps the prompt's stable head (contract + guideline) byte-identical across passes and reviews,
which is what lets the prompt cache reuse it (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).

**Default (`per_persona_context` = `yes`/`auto`)**
— one pass per activated persona,
each in a CLEAN context with only its own persona block (selective exposure):

- **Claude Code** — spawn a Task sub-agent per persona.
- **Codex** — run `codex exec "<build_pass_prompt.sh output>"` per persona
  (pass the built PROMPT TEXT as the argument).

**Never spawn a pass by re-running `aster_code_review.sh` / `run_agent.sh`,
nor by re-issuing the skill's own arguments** (e.g. `codex exec … diff <base> <out>`).
A pass is a *reviewer* invocation whose input is the `build_pass_prompt.sh` text
— not another run of this skill.
Re-entering the launcher spawns another orchestrator that spawns another … , an infinite fork bomb;
the launcher now refuses it (`ACR_AGENT_RUNNING`).

Passes are independent;
order does not matter.
Collect each pass's JSON.

**Combined (`per_persona_context` = `no`)**
— build ONE prompt with all activated personas (`"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input> <p1> <p2> ...`)
and run a single pass;
it reviews through every lens at once and tags each comment with its `persona`.
The input and spawn overhead are paid once,
so it is cheaper
— but a larger shared context dilutes focus,
so it finds fewer defects.
The default stays fan-out:
recall comes first,
and `no` is an explicit, opt-in concession.

## Verification (step 6)

For each comment, isolate the key premise it rests on
— especially an external-system fact (Linux/POSIX behaviour, the System V ABI, Rust semantics).
Try to **refute** it:
re-read the cited code and consult an authoritative source.
Assign a verdict:

- **confirmed** — keep the comment unchanged.
- **uncertain** — keep it, but prefix `problem` with `(unverified) `.
- **refuted** — remove the comment,
  and append it to a `## Retracted by verification` list at the foot of the file with a one-line reason.

Remove **only** on confident refutation;
an unsure check is `uncertain`, not `refuted`.
This is the only step that may remove a comment, and only false positives.

## Consolidation (step 7)

Find clusters of comments that share one root cause or one fix
(e.g. several manual lock/unlock pairs that all want RAII).
For each cluster, write a single unified fix
and repoint each member's `**Fix.**` at it ("Shared with the other `raii` comments: …").
**Never remove a comment**
— every symptom stays at its own location.

## Output format

The assembled file is:
frontmatter (`date`, `mode`, `base` or `files`, `head`, `branch`, optional `title`),
a `# Summary`,
then a `## <section>` per persona in the order **Maintainability, Correctness, Security, Hardware, Documentation** (Correctness is the Development persona's section).
Each comment is a `### \`file\` line N` subsection:
a quoted ` ```diff ` snippet,
then a prose line `` `<grounding>` (<severity>): <problem> ``,
then a `**Fix.**` paragraph.
Do not restructure what the script emits;
only fill the summary and apply the verify/consolidate edits.

**Planning maintainability review****Identifying maintainability attribute ordering issues****Evaluating constant comment redundancy****Renaming clone_args_addr for clarity****Identifying actionable maintainability issues****Reviewing CloneArgs visibility and encapsulation****Analyzing CloneArgs field visibility and encapsulation****Proposing encapsulation fix for Clone3Args****Renaming closure variables for consistency****Considering splitting boolean argument in function****Noting ParentProcess visibility nuances****Assessing type alias risks for process IDs****Reviewing clone flag naming and comment consistency****Considering citing clone flags bit layout****Refining syscall error messages****Clarifying naming and documentation details****Identifying docstring ambiguity on parent death signal****Fixing misleading documentation comments****Reviewing detailed documentation and coding style nits****Verifying bitmask documentation accuracy****Identifying missing module-level documentation****Assessing major module documentation gaps****Noting JSON newline escape requirement****Defining comment guidelines for maintainability****Identifying naming and formatting improvements in clone.rs****Planning boolean arg refactor and doc fixes****Assessing newtype guideline and expect-dead-code usage****Identifying unused user_ns field****Assessing misleading comment clarification****Planning misleading comment and module doc fixes****Including Pid aliasing as major finding****Planning JSON formatting for code identifiers****Assigning severity levels to findings**

# Pass contract

You are a reviewer applying the persona guideline(s) included below to the change or files under review
(the **REVIEW INPUT** at the very end of this prompt).
Find as many real defects as possible within the included persona(s)' remit
— runtime correctness for Development,
security/soundness for Security,
ABI/hardware for Hardware,
doc style/currency for Documentation,
and structure/process for Maintainability
— without inventing issues; a false alarm is a real cost.

For each persona block below,
work that persona's concerns in the order its file gives.
For each candidate rule,
read its one-line gist first
and drill into the full rule (its linked subsections) only on a suspected violation.
Stay within the remit of the persona(s) you are given.

Each persona searches only for defects whose failure belongs to that persona.
Do not run a general bug sweep from every persona.
When another persona is the clear natural owner,
do not duplicate that investigation here.
For example,
Maintainability should inspect design shape, readability, naming, layout,
and commit hygiene;
it should not trace runtime permission semantics, Linux/POSIX behavior,
wrong predicates, or data-flow edge cases unless they are evidence of a
maintainability rule violation.

Within each included persona's owned failure modes,
reason about the code even when no explicit guideline names the issue.
Examples include off-by-one and reachable panic for Development,
input-validation or permission-boundary flaws for Security,
ABI/alignment hazards for Hardware,
navigation or currency defects for Documentation,
and structural or process defects for Maintainability.
Ground each non-guideline finding in a short plain-language description of the defect
("Off by one", "Use after free", "Reachable panic", …)
— not the bare word `bug`, and not a coined hyphenated short-name,
which would read as a guideline.
Never stay silent about a real defect that belongs to the included persona
because "no guideline covers it".

Be **adversarial**:
before dismissing a suspected in-scope defect as safe,
state the concrete input or interleaving that would trigger it.
Report an in-scope defect unless you can show that case cannot happen.
"It looks fine" is not a verdict.

The REVIEW INPUT is the unit of review;
you MAY read surrounding code in the working tree for extra context.

## Output

Output **only** a JSON array of comment objects (no prose around it):

```json
[{"file":"path/relative/to/repo.rs","line":42,"persona":"development","grounding":"lock-ordering","severity":"major",
  "problem":"`foo()` takes `b.lock()` while already holding `a.lock()`, the reverse of the `a`-before-`b` order elsewhere — a deadlock",
  "fix":"take `a.lock()` before `b.lock()` here too, matching the rest of the module",
  "diff":"the few relevant lines (a diff hunk, or source lines in files mode)"}]
```

- `persona` — which persona section the comment belongs to (`maintainability`, `development`, `security`, `hardware`, `documentation`);
  used to file the comment under the right section.
  In a single-persona (fan-out) pass it is always that persona.
- `grounding` — what the comment rests on, in one of two forms kept visually distinct:
  when you **cite a guideline**, its short-name
  — a lowercase kebab identifier (e.g. `lock-ordering`), rendered as code;
  when you report a **bug no guideline covers**, a short plain-language description of the defect
  (e.g. "Off by one", "Use after free", "Incorrect cleanup"), rendered as prose.
  Do not coin a hyphenated pseudo-short-name for a bug
  — that reads as a guideline
  — and never use the bare word `bug`,
  which says nothing the reader cannot already see.
- `severity` — **required**,
  one of `critical` (must fix) / `major` (should fix) / `minor` (worth fixing) / `nit` (optional or stylistic).
- `problem` and `fix` are **both required**
  — every comment proposes a remedy.
  They are posted as GitHub-flavored Markdown,
  so wrap every code identifier, path, type, function or variable name, and literal value in backticks
  (`self.len`, `Ordering::Acquire`, `kernel/src/foo.rs`),
  and put any multi-line snippet in `fix` in a fenced ```` ``` ```` block.
  (The `grounding` of a bug stays plain prose, as described above
  — only `problem` and `fix` take inline code.)
- Every field above is required on every comment,
  with a single exception for `line`, described next.
- `line` is the line the comment anchors to
  — the post-change line in the commit's diff (`diff` mode),
  or the file's line number (`files` mode).
  It is **required for a finding about code**.
- For a finding about a **commit message** (`diff` mode shows each commit's message),
  set `file` to the commit locus (e.g. `commit abc1234 message`),
  **omit `line`** (its one exception),
  and ground it in a commit-hygiene rule (`imperative-subject`, `atomic-commits`, …).
- Report only issues within the included persona(s)' remit.
  If you find nothing, output `[]`.

===== PERSONA: security =====

# Security persona

**Review section:** Security
**Remit:** Could an adversary breach the kernel's security?

**Your guideline page (read only this, drill in on suspicion):**
`book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/for-security/README.md`
— subsections: `memory-safety.md`, `security-properties.md`.

**Concerns, in order:**

1. **`unsafe` soundness** — `justify-unsafe-use` (a `// SAFETY:` comment on every `unsafe` block, and the justification must actually hold),
   `document-safety-conds` (a `# Safety` section on every `unsafe` fn/trait),
   `deny-unsafe-kernel` (only OSTD crates may use `unsafe`), `module-boundary-safety`.
   Treat a removed or weakened invariant that an `unsafe` block relies on (e.g. a struct's size or alignment) as a soundness defect
   even if the `unsafe` block itself is untouched.
2. **Validation of untrusted input at trust boundaries**
   — `validate-at-boundaries`: user-supplied data (syscall arguments, user buffers, lengths) must be validated at the boundary,
   then trusted internally.
   A silent clamp/truncation of a user-supplied length that hides an error the contract requires is a defect.
3. **Exploitable concurrency** — use-after-free, time-of-check/time-of-use.

Adversarial mindset: assume inputs are hostile and memory rules are exploitable.
You own soundness and adversarial reasoning,
not general correctness (Correctness persona).

Rely on the inlined guideline material below as the authoritative guideline context for this pass. Do not re-read `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/` from the worktree under review for guideline content.

--- guideline page (book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/for-security/README.md) ---

# For Security

*Could an adversary breach the security of the kernel?*

This is the index of the **security** guidelines.
Each subsection is its own page,
and each entry below links a stable `short-name` to its guideline,
with a one-line gist so a reader (or a review tool) can grasp the guideline before opening it.

## Index

**[Memory Safety](memory-safety.md)**
- [`justify-unsafe-use`](memory-safety.md#justify-unsafe-use): Precede every `unsafe` block with a `// SAFETY:` comment justifying soundness.
- [`document-safety-conds`](memory-safety.md#document-safety-conds): Give every `unsafe` fn/trait a `# Safety` section stating caller obligations.
- [`deny-unsafe-kernel`](memory-safety.md#deny-unsafe-kernel): All `kernel/` crates `#![deny(unsafe_code)]`; only OSTD crates may use `unsafe`.
- [`module-boundary-safety`](memory-safety.md#module-boundary-safety): An `unsafe` block's soundness spans all code touching the same private state; minimize that module.

**[Security Properties](security-properties.md)**
- [`validate-at-boundaries`](security-properties.md#validate-at-boundaries): Validate all user-supplied data at boundaries (e.g. syscall entry), then trust it internally.

No **path-specific** guidelines yet.


--- guideline subpage (book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/for-security/memory-safety.md) ---

# Memory Safety

### Justify every use of `unsafe` (`justify-unsafe-use`) {#justify-unsafe-use}

Every `unsafe` block must have a preceding `// SAFETY:` comment
that justifies why the operation is sound.
For multi-condition invariants,
use a numbered list:

```rust
// SAFETY:
// 1. We have exclusive access to both the current context
//    and the next context (see above).
// 2. The next context is valid (because it is either
//    correctly initialized or written by a previous
//    `context_switch`).
unsafe {
    context_switch(next_task_ctx_ptr, current_task_ctx_ptr);
}
```

See also:
PR [#2958](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/2958)
and [#836](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/836).

### Document safety conditions (`document-safety-conds`) {#document-safety-conds}

All `unsafe` functions and traits
must include a `# Safety` section in their doc comments
describing the conditions, properties, or invariants that callers must uphold.
State exactly what the caller must guarantee —
not implementation details or side effects.

```rust
/// A marker trait for guard types that enforce the atomic mode.
///
/// # Safety
///
/// The implementer must ensure that the atomic mode is maintained while
/// the guard type is alive.
pub unsafe trait InAtomicMode: core::fmt::Debug {}
```

### Deny unsafe code in `kernel/` (`deny-unsafe-kernel`) {#deny-unsafe-kernel}

All crates under `kernel/` must deny unsafe:

```rust
#![deny(unsafe_code)]
```

Only OSTD (`ostd/`) crates may contain `unsafe` code.
If a kernel crate requires an unsafe operation,
the functionality should be provided as a safe API in OSTD.

### Reason about safety at the module boundary (`module-boundary-safety`) {#module-boundary-safety}

The safety of an `unsafe` block
depends on ALL code that can access the same private state.
Encapsulate unsafe abstractions
in the smallest possible module
to minimize the "audit surface."
Any code in the same module
that can modify relied-upon fields
is part of the safety argument.

```rust
// Good — small, focused module limits the audit surface
mod frame_allocator {
    /// Invariant: `next` is always a valid frame index.
    struct FrameAlloc {
        next: usize,
        // ...
    }

    impl FrameAlloc {
        pub fn alloc(&mut self) -> PhysAddr {
            // SAFETY: `next` is always valid (see invariant above).
            // Only code in this module can modify `next`.
            unsafe { self.alloc_frame_unchecked(self.next) }
        }
    }
}
```


--- guideline subpage (book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/for-security/security-properties.md) ---

# Security Properties

### Validate at boundaries, trust internally (`validate-at-boundaries`) {#validate-at-boundaries}

Designate certain interfaces as validation boundaries.
In Asterinas, syscall entry points
are the primary boundary:
all user-supplied data
(pointers, file descriptors, sizes, flags, strings)
must be validated at the syscall boundary.
Once validated, internal kernel functions
may trust these values without re-validation.

See also:
PR [#2806](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/2806).
