/// Sends `SIGKILL` to all other threads in the current process.
///
/// This is only needed when initiating an `exit_group` for the first time.
fn sigkill_other_threads(current_task: &CurrentTask, task_set: &TaskSet) {
    for task in task_set.as_slice() {
        if core::ptr::eq(current_task.as_ref(), task.as_ref()) {
            continue;
        }
        task.as_posix_thread()
            .unwrap()
            .enqueue_signal(Box::new(KernelSignal::new(SIGKILL)));
    }
}

/// Writes zero to `clear_child_tid` and performs a futex wake.
fn wake_clear_ctid(thread_local: &ThreadLocal) {
    let clear_ctid = thread_local.clear_child_tid().get();

    if clear_ctid == 0 {
        return;
    }

    let _ = current_userspace!()
        .write_val(clear_ctid, &0u32)
        .inspect_err(|err| debug!("exit: cannot clear the child TID: {:?}", err));
    let _ = futex_wake(clear_ctid, 1, None)
        .inspect_err(|err| debug!("exit: cannot wake the futex on the child TID: {:?}", err));

    thread_local.clear_child_tid().set(0);
}

/// Walks the robust futex list, marking futex dead and waking waiters.
///
/// This corresponds to Linux's `exit_robust_list`. Errors are silently ignored.
fn wake_robust_list(thread_local: &ThreadLocal, tid: Tid) {
    let mut robust_list = thread_local.robust_list().borrow_mut();

    let list_head = match *robust_list {
        Some(robust_list_head) => robust_list_head,
        None => return,
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

use crate::{
    prelude::*,
    process::{CloneFlags, ContextUnshareAdminApi},
    syscall::SyscallReturn,
};

pub fn sys_unshare(unshare_flags: u32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    let mut flags = CloneFlags::from_bits(unshare_flags)
        .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid `unshare` flags"))?;
    debug!("unshare flags = {:?}", flags);

    apply_implied_flags(&mut flags);
    check_flags(flags, ctx)?;

    if flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_FILES) {
        ctx.unshare_files();
    }

    if flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_FS) {
        ctx.unshare_fs();
    }

    if flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SYSVSEM) {
        ctx.unshare_sysvsem();
    }

    let ns_flags = flags.intersection(CloneFlags::CLONE_NS_FLAGS);
    if !ns_flags.is_empty() {
        ctx.unshare_namespaces(ns_flags)?;
    }

    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
}

fn apply_implied_flags(flags: &mut CloneFlags) {
    if flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWUSER) {
        *flags |= CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD | CloneFlags::CLONE_FS;
    }

    if flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND) {
        *flags |= CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD;
    }

    if flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS) {
        *flags |= CloneFlags::CLONE_FS;
    }
}

fn check_flags(flags: CloneFlags, ctx: &Context) -> Result<()> {
    const VALID_FLAGS: CloneFlags = CloneFlags::CLONE_NS_FLAGS
        .union(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS)
        .union(CloneFlags::CLONE_FILES)
        .union(CloneFlags::CLONE_FS)
        .union(CloneFlags::CLONE_SYSVSEM)
        .union(CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD)
        .union(CloneFlags::CLONE_VM)
        .union(CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND);

    let invalid_flags = flags - VALID_FLAGS;
    if !invalid_flags.is_empty() {
        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "unsupported `unshare` flags");
    }

    if flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD | CloneFlags::CLONE_VM | CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND)
        && ctx.process.tasks().lock().as_slice().len() != 1
    {
        return_errno_with_message!(
            Errno::EINVAL,
            "`CLONE_THREAD`, `CLONE_VM`, and `CLONE_SIGHAND` can be specified only if the process is single-threaded"
        );
    }

    if flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_VM) && ctx.user_space().is_vmar_shared() {
        return_errno_with_message!(
            Errno::EINVAL,
            "`CLONE_VM` can only be used when the VMAR is not shared"
        );
    }

    Ok(())
}

// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

#![expect(dead_code)]

use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU8, Ordering};

use super::constants::*;
use crate::prelude::*;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SigNum {
    sig_num: u8,
}

impl TryFrom<u8> for SigNum {
    type Error = Error;

    fn try_from(sig_num: u8) -> Result<Self> {
        if !(MIN_STD_SIG_NUM..=MAX_RT_SIG_NUM).contains(&sig_num) {
            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid signal number");
        }
        Ok(SigNum { sig_num })
    }
}

impl SigNum {
    /// Caller must ensure the sig_num is valid. Otherwise, use try_from will check sig_num and does not panic.
    pub const fn from_u8(sig_num: u8) -> Self {
        if sig_num > MAX_RT_SIG_NUM || sig_num < MIN_STD_SIG_NUM {
            panic!("invalid signal number")
        }
        SigNum { sig_num }
    }

    pub const fn as_u8(&self) -> u8 {
        self.sig_num
    }

    pub fn is_std(&self) -> bool {
        self.sig_num <= MAX_STD_SIG_NUM
    }

    pub fn is_real_time(&self) -> bool {
        self.sig_num >= MIN_RT_SIG_NUM
    }

    pub const fn sig_name(&self) -> &'static str {
        match *self {
            SIGHUP => "SIGHUP",
            SIGINT => "SIGINT",
            SIGQUIT => "SIGQUIT",
            SIGILL => "SIGILL",
            SIGTRAP => "SIGTRAP",
            SIGABRT => "SIGABRT",
            SIGBUS => "SIGBUS",

 succeeded in 0ms:

impl CloneArgs {
    /// Prepares a new [`CloneArgs`] based on the arguments for clone(2).
    pub fn for_clone(
        raw_flags: u64,
        parent_tid: Vaddr,
        child_tid: Vaddr,
        tls: u64,
        stack: u64,
    ) -> Result<Self> {
        const FLAG_MASK: u64 = 0xff;
        let flags = CloneFlags::from(raw_flags & !FLAG_MASK);
        if flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS | CloneFlags::CLONE_FS) {
            return_errno_with_message!(
                Errno::EINVAL,
                "CLONE_NEWNS cannot be used with CLONE_FS for clone syscall"
            );
        }

        let exit_signal = raw_flags & FLAG_MASK;
        // Disambiguate the `parent_tid` parameter. The field is used
        // both for `CLONE_PIDFD` and `CLONE_PARENT_SETTID`, so at
        // most only one can be specified.
        let (pidfd, parent_tid) = match (
            flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PIDFD),
            flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT_SETTID),
        ) {
            (false, false) => (None, None),
            (true, false) => (Some(parent_tid), None),
            (false, true) => (None, Some(parent_tid)),
            (true, true) => {
                return_errno_with_message!(
                    Errno::EINVAL,
                    "CLONE_PIDFD was specified with CLONE_PARENT_SETTID"
                );
            }
        };

        Ok(Self {
            flags,
            pidfd,
            child_tid,
            parent_tid,
            exit_signal: (exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(exit_signal as u8)),
            stack,
            tls,
            ..Default::default()
        })
    }

    pub fn for_fork() -> Self {
        Self {
            exit_signal: Some(SIGCHLD),
            ..Default::default()
        }
    }

    pub fn for_vfork() -> Self {
        Self {
            flags: CloneFlags::CLONE_VFORK | CloneFlags::CLONE_VM,
            exit_signal: Some(SIGCHLD),

impl TryFrom<Clone3Args> for CloneArgs {
    type Error = Error;

    fn try_from(value: Clone3Args) -> Result<Self> {
        // TODO: Deal with set_tid, set_tid_size, cgroup
        if value.set_tid != 0 || value.set_tid_size != 0 {
            warn!("set_tid is not supported");
        }

        if value.cgroup != 0 {
            warn!("cgroup is not supported");
        }

        let flags = CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)
            .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid clone flags"))?;
        let exit_signal =
            (value.exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(value.exit_signal as u8));

        if flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT | CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD)
            && exit_signal.is_some()
        {
            return_errno_with_message!(
                Errno::EINVAL,
                "`CLONE_PARENT` and  `CLONE_THREAD` cannot be used if the exit signal is specified"
            )
        }

        Ok(Self {
            flags,
            pidfd: Some(value.pidfd as Vaddr),
            child_tid: value.child_tid as _,
            parent_tid: Some(value.parent_tid as _),
            exit_signal,
            stack: value.stack,
            stack_size: NonZeroU64::new(value.stack_size),
            tls: value.tls,
            _set_tid: Some(value.set_tid),
            _set_tid_size: Some(value.set_tid_size),
            _cgroup: Some(value.cgroup),
        })
    }
}

fn clone_vm(parent_process_vm: &ProcessVm, clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> Result<ProcessVm> {
    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_VM) {
        Ok(parent_process_vm.clone())
    } else {
        ProcessVm::fork_from(parent_process_vm)
    }
}

fn clone_user_ctx(
    parent_context: &UserContext,
    new_sp: u64,
    stack_size: Option<NonZeroU64>,
    tls: u64,
    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
) -> UserContext {
    let mut child_context = parent_context.clone();
    // The return value of child thread is zero
    child_context.set_syscall_ret(0);

    if new_sp != 0 {
        // If stack size is not 0, the `new_sp` points to the BOTTOMMOST byte of stack.
        if let Some(size) = stack_size {
            child_context.set_stack_pointer((new_sp + size.get()) as usize)
        }
        // If stack size is 0, the new_sp points to the TOPMOST byte of stack.
        else {
            child_context.set_stack_pointer(new_sp as usize);
        }
    }
    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SETTLS) {
        child_context.set_tls_pointer(tls as usize);
    }

    child_context
}

fn clone_fs(parent_fs: &Arc<ThreadFsInfo>, clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> Arc<ThreadFsInfo> {
    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_FS) {
        parent_fs.clone()
    } else {
        Arc::new(parent_fs.as_ref().clone())
    }
}

fn clone_files(parent_file_table: &RwArc<FileTable>, clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> RwArc<FileTable> {
    // if CLONE_FILES is set, the child and parent shares the same file table
    // Otherwise, the child will deep copy a new file table.
    // FIXME: the clone may not be deep copy.
    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_FILES) {
        parent_file_table.clone()
    } else {
        RwArc::new(parent_file_table.read().clone())
    }
}

fn clone_sighand(
    parent_sig_dispositions: &Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>>,
    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
) -> Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>> {
    // similar to CLONE_FILES
    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND) {
        parent_sig_dispositions.clone()
    } else {
        Arc::new(Mutex::new(*parent_sig_dispositions.lock()))
    }
}

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 108

> ```diff

`information-hiding` (major): `CloneArgs` is documented as an internal homogenized representation, but every field is `pub`, including unchecked ABI details such as `_set_tid`, `_set_tid_size`, and `_cgroup`. That exposes the representation to the whole crate and lets later call sites construct states that bypass the checked constructors.

**Fix.** Keep `CloneArgs` fields private and expose checked constructors or accessors. Move `TryFrom<Clone3Args>` into `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` or add a `CloneArgs::for_clone3(...)` constructor so `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` does not need field access.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 163

> ```diff
> 139	let exit_signal = raw_flags & FLAG_MASK;
> ...
> 163	exit_signal: (exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(exit_signal as u8)),
> ```

Reachable panic (critical): `CloneArgs::for_clone` converts the user-controlled low byte of `raw_flags` with `SigNum::from_u8`. A call such as `clone(0xff, ...)` reaches the `panic!` in `SigNum::from_u8` instead of returning `EINVAL`, so an invalid exit signal can panic the kernel.

**Fix.** Validate the signal with `SigNum::try_from` and propagate `EINVAL` before constructing `CloneArgs`, for example by replacing the field expression with a checked `Option<SigNum>` value.

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 579

> ```diff
> 576	if new_sp != 0 {
> 577	    // If stack size is not 0, the `new_sp` points to the BOTTOMMOST byte of stack.
> 578	    if let Some(size) = stack_size {
> 579	        child_context.set_stack_pointer((new_sp + size.get()) as usize)
> 580	    }
> ```

`checked-arithmetic` (major): `clone_user_ctx` adds the user-provided `new_sp` and `stack_size` with unchecked arithmetic. With `clone3({ stack: u64::MAX - 3, stack_size: 8, ... })`, the stack pointer wraps to a low address in release builds instead of rejecting the invalid stack range.

**Fix.** Use `checked_add` for `new_sp + size.get()` and return `EOVERFLOW` or `EINVAL` on overflow. That likely means making `clone_user_ctx` return `Result<UserContext>` and propagating the error at its call sites.

 succeeded in 134ms:

    pub fn sig_dispositions(&self) -> &Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>> {
        &self.sig_dispositions
    }

    /// Enqueues a process-directed signal.
    ///
    /// This method should only be used for enqueue kernel signals and fault signals.
    ///
    /// The signal may be delivered to any one of the threads that does not currently have the
    /// signal blocked. If more than one of the threads have the signal unblocked, then this method
    /// chooses an arbitrary thread to which to deliver the signal.
    //
    // TODO: Restrict this method with the access control tool.
    pub fn enqueue_signal(&self, signal: impl Signal + Clone + 'static) {
        if self.status.is_zombie() {
            return;
        }

        let sig_dispositions = self.sig_dispositions.lock();

        // Drop the signal if it's ignored. See explanation at `enqueue_signal_locked`.
        let signum = signal.num();
        if sig_dispositions.get(signum).will_ignore(signum) {
            return;
        }

        let threads = self.tasks.lock();

        // Enqueue the signal to the first thread that does not block the signal.
        for thread in threads.as_slice() {
            let posix_thread = thread.as_posix_thread().unwrap();
            if !posix_thread.has_signal_blocked(signal.num()) {
                posix_thread.enqueue_signal_locked(Box::new(signal), sig_dispositions);
                return;
            }
        }

        // If all threads block the signal, enqueue the signal to the main thread.
        let thread = threads.main();
        let posix_thread = thread.as_posix_thread().unwrap();
        posix_thread.enqueue_signal_locked(Box::new(signal), sig_dispositions);
    }

    /// Clears the parent death signal.
    pub fn clear_parent_death_signal(&self) {
        self.parent_death_signal.clear();
    }

    /// Sets the parent death signal as `signum`.
    pub fn set_parent_death_signal(&self, sig_num: SigNum) {
        self.parent_death_signal.set(sig_num);
    }

    /// Returns the parent death signal.
    ///
    /// The parent death signal is the signal will be sent to child processes
    /// when the process exits.
    pub fn parent_death_signal(&self) -> Option<SigNum> {
        self.parent_death_signal.as_sig_num()
    }

    pub fn set_exit_signal(&self, sig_num: SigNum) {
        self.exit_signal.set(sig_num);
    }

    pub fn exit_signal(&self) -> Option<SigNum> {
        self.exit_signal.as_sig_num()
    }

    /// Returns a reference to the process status.
    pub fn status(&self) -> &ProcessStatus {
        &self.status

**Fix.** Use `checked_add` for `new_sp + size.get()` and return `EOVERFLOW` or `EINVAL` on overflow. That likely means making `clone_user_ctx` return `Result<UserContext>` and propagating the error at its call sites.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 89

> ```diff
> 88	// TODO: Deal with set_tid, set_tid_size, cgroup
> 89	if value.set_tid != 0 || value.set_tid_size != 0 {
> 90	    warn!("set_tid is not supported");
> 91	}
> 92

> 94	    warn!("cgroup is not supported");
> 95	}

Unsupported request ignored (major): `clone3` only logs nonzero `set_tid`, `set_tid_size`, and `cgroup`, then continues. A caller requesting `set_tid_size = 1` gets a child with an arbitrary allocated PID, and a caller passing `cgroup` gets a child outside the requested cgroup while the syscall still reports success.

**Fix.** Until these fields are implemented, reject nonzero `set_tid`, `set_tid_size`, or `cgroup` with an error such as `EINVAL` instead of silently ignoring the requested semantics.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 97

> ```diff
> 97	let flags = CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)
> 98	    .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid clone flags"))?;
> ```

Invalid flags accepted (major): `CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)` truncates the upper 32 bits of the `clone3` `flags` field before validation. For example, `flags = 0x1_0000_0000` is accepted as `CloneFlags::empty()` and creates a child instead of rejecting the unknown flag.

**Fix.** Reject nonzero upper bits before converting, for example with `u32::try_from(value.flags)` mapped to `EINVAL`, then pass the checked `u32` to `CloneFlags::from_bits`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 100

> ```diff
> 99	let exit_signal =
> 100	    (value.exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(value.exit_signal as u8));
> ```

Reachable panic (critical): `CloneArgs::try_from` casts the user-controlled `clone3` `exit_signal` to `u8` and calls `SigNum::from_u8`. An input such as `exit_signal = 256` is nonzero, truncates to `0`, and reaches the `panic!` in `SigNum::from_u8` instead of returning `EINVAL`.

**Fix.** Validate `value.exit_signal` without truncation: reject values that do not fit in `u8`, then call `SigNum::try_from` and propagate its `EINVAL`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 102

> ```diff
> 97	let flags = CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)
> 98	    .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid clone flags"))?;
> 99	let exit_signal =
> 100	    (value.exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(value.exit_signal as u8));
> 101	
> 102	if flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT | CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD)
> 103	    && exit_signal.is_some()
> ```

Invalid flag combination accepted (major): `clone3` does not reject `CLONE_NEWNS | CLONE_FS`, although `CloneArgs::for_clone` rejects the same combination for `clone`. With both flags set, `clone_fs` shares the caller's `ThreadFsInfo`, then the `CLONE_NEWNS` path calls `switch_to_mnt_ns` through that shared object, so a successful privileged `clone3` can switch the parent's filesystem resolver too.

**Fix.** Add the same `CLONE_NEWNS | CLONE_FS` validation to `CloneArgs::try_from` or to `CloneArgs::check`, returning `EINVAL` before any child state is created.

### `kernel/src/thread/task.rs` line 61

> ```diff
> 54	// The `clone` syscall may require child process to write the thread pid to the specified address.
> 55	// Make sure the store operation completes before the clone call returns control to user space
> 56	// in the child process.
> 57	let child_tid_ptr = current_thread_local.set_child_tid().get();
> 58	if is_userspace_vaddr(child_tid_ptr) {
> 59	    current_userspace!()
> 60	        .write_val(child_tid_ptr, &current_posix_thread.tid())
> 61	        .unwrap();
> 62	}
> ```

`propagate-errors` (critical): `CLONE_CHILD_SETTID` stores a user-provided pointer in `set_child_tid`, and the child task later writes through it with `.unwrap()`. A pointer that is in the userspace range but unmapped or read-only makes `write_val` return an error, so `clone(CLONE_CHILD_SETTID, child_tidptr = bad_addr, ...)` can panic the kernel in the child entry path.

**Fix.** Handle the `write_val` error explicitly instead of unwrapping. Since this runs after the child task starts, either arrange for the checked write to happen before scheduling the child, or terminate/signal the child on `EFAULT` according to the intended Linux-compatible behavior.

## Security

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 163

> ```diff
>     139        let exit_signal = raw_flags & FLAG_MASK;
> ...
>     163            exit_signal: (exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(exit_signal as u8)),
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `raw_flags` is a syscall argument, but `CloneArgs::for_clone` converts the low byte with `SigNum::from_u8` instead of validating it. A hostile caller can invoke `clone()` with an exit signal such as `65`; `SigNum::from_u8(65)` panics, turning invalid user input into a kernel panic instead of `EINVAL`.

**Fix.** Validate the user-supplied signal at the syscall boundary and propagate `EINVAL`, for example by using `SigNum::try_from(exit_signal as u8)?` after checking `exit_signal != 0`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 97

> ```diff
>      97        let flags = CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)
>      98            .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid clone flags"))?;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `clone3()` accepts `flags` as a user-supplied `u64`, but this code narrows it with `value.flags as u32` before validation. A caller can set any high 32 flag bits and they are silently discarded, so invalid user input is accepted as a different internal `CloneFlags` value instead of being rejected at the syscall boundary.

**Fix.** Reject any `flags` value that cannot be represented exactly as the supported `CloneFlags` bitmask before constructing `CloneFlags`, for example by checking the upper 32 bits and then using `CloneFlags::from_bits` on the validated value.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 100

> ```diff
>      99        let exit_signal =
>     100            (value.exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(value.exit_signal as u8));
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `exit_signal` comes directly from the user-provided `clone_args`, but `CloneArgs::try_from` truncates it to `u8` and calls `SigNum::from_u8`. Inputs such as `65` panic, and inputs such as `0x1_0000_0011` are silently accepted as `SIGCHLD`, so invalid user input is not validated at the syscall boundary.

**Fix.** Require `value.exit_signal` to be either `0` or a valid signal number, and build it with `SigNum::try_from` instead of `SigNum::from_u8`.

## Documentation

> 250             | CloneFlags::CLONE_VFORK

> 252             | CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT;
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): `CloneFlags::check_unsupported_flags()` now treats `CLONE_NEWNS` as supported for `clone` and `clone3`, which enhances the user-visible syscall flag surface, but the Linux Compatibility docs in `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/` do not document this newly supported namespace flag or its constraints such as the `CLONE_NEWNS` plus `CLONE_FS` rejection.

**Fix.** Update the Linux Compatibility syscall coverage for `clone` and `clone3` in the same change, including the relevant Markdown coverage page and SCML coverage data, to list `CLONE_NEWNS` as supported and document the unsupported/invalid combinations.

**Fix.** Update the Linux Compatibility syscall coverage for `clone` and `clone3`, including the matching SCML coverage, to include `CLONE_PARENT` and its invalid combinations/limitations.

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The process cloning code is structured around a useful common `CloneArgs` representation, and the review found several places where the implementation is already explicit about Linux flag constraints. The highest-risk issues are all at the syscall boundary: user-controlled signal numbers are converted with `SigNum::from_u8` and can panic, `clone3` truncates 64-bit flags before validation, unsupported `clone3` fields are accepted after a warning, and `CLONE_CHILD_SETTID` can unwrap an `EFAULT` in the child task entry path.
+
+The next fixes should focus on making `CloneArgs` construction the single checked boundary for both `clone` and `clone3`, validating signals and flag widths before storing them, rejecting unsupported `clone3` fields until implemented, and propagating child TID write errors without panic. Structurally, `CloneArgs` should hide its fields behind checked constructors, and the Linux compatibility docs should be updated for the newly supported `CLONE_NEWNS` and `CLONE_PARENT` flag surface.

# Summary

The process cloning code is structured around a useful common `CloneArgs` representation, and the review found several places where the implementation is already explicit about Linux flag constraints. The highest-risk issues are all at the syscall boundary: user-controlled signal numbers are converted with `SigNum::from_u8` and can panic, `clone3` truncates 64-bit flags before validation, unsupported `clone3` fields are accepted after a warning, and `CLONE_CHILD_SETTID` can unwrap an `EFAULT` in the child task entry path.

The next fixes should focus on making `CloneArgs` construction the single checked boundary for both `clone` and `clone3`, validating signals and flag widths before storing them, rejecting unsupported `clone3` fields until implemented, and propagating child TID write errors without panic. Structurally, `CloneArgs` should hide its fields behind checked constructors, and the Linux compatibility docs should be updated for the newly supported `CLONE_NEWNS` and `CLONE_PARENT` flag surface.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 106

> ```diff
> 106	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default)]
> 107	pub struct CloneArgs {
> ```

`alphabetical-attrs` (nit): `CloneArgs` derives `Debug`, `Clone`, `Copy`, and `Default` out of the required alphabetical order, which makes attribute diffs inconsistent with the local Rust-specific convention.

**Fix.** Sort the derived traits as `Clone`, `Copy`, `Debug`, `Default`.

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 108

> ```diff

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The process cloning code is structured around a useful common `CloneArgs` representation, and the review found several places where the implementation is already explicit about Linux flag constraints. The highest-risk issues are all at the syscall boundary: user-controlled signal numbers are converted with `SigNum::from_u8` and can panic, `clone3` truncates 64-bit flags before validation, unsupported `clone3` fields are accepted after a warning, and `CLONE_CHILD_SETTID` can unwrap an `EFAULT` in the child task entry path.
+
+The next fixes should focus on making `CloneArgs` construction the single checked boundary for both `clone` and `clone3`, validating signals and flag widths before storing them, rejecting unsupported `clone3` fields until implemented, and propagating child TID write errors without panic. Structurally, `CloneArgs` should hide its fields behind checked constructors, and the Linux compatibility docs should be updated for the newly supported `CLONE_NEWNS` and `CLONE_PARENT` flag surface.
 
 ## Maintainability
 
@@ -203,7 +205,7 @@
 
 Reachable panic (critical): `CloneArgs::for_clone` converts the user-controlled low byte of `raw_flags` with `SigNum::from_u8`. A call such as `clone(0xff, ...)` reaches the `panic!` in `SigNum::from_u8` instead of returning `EINVAL`, so an invalid exit signal can panic the kernel.
 
-**Fix.** Validate the signal with `SigNum::try_from` and propagate `EINVAL` before constructing `CloneArgs`, for example by replacing the field expression with a checked `Option<SigNum>` value.
+**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding `validate-at-boundaries` comment: make `CloneArgs` construction validate exit signals with `SigNum::try_from` and propagate `EINVAL` before storing `Option<SigNum>`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 579
 
@@ -245,7 +247,7 @@
 
 Invalid flags accepted (major): `CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)` truncates the upper 32 bits of the `clone3` `flags` field before validation. For example, `flags = 0x1_0000_0000` is accepted as `CloneFlags::empty()` and creates a child instead of rejecting the unknown flag.
 
-**Fix.** Reject nonzero upper bits before converting, for example with `u32::try_from(value.flags)` mapped to `EINVAL`, then pass the checked `u32` to `CloneFlags::from_bits`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding `validate-at-boundaries` comment: reject nonzero upper bits before converting, for example with `u32::try_from(value.flags)` mapped to `EINVAL`, then pass the checked `u32` to `CloneFlags::from_bits`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 100
 
@@ -256,7 +258,7 @@
 
 Reachable panic (critical): `CloneArgs::try_from` casts the user-controlled `clone3` `exit_signal` to `u8` and calls `SigNum::from_u8`. An input such as `exit_signal = 256` is nonzero, truncates to `0`, and reaches the `panic!` in `SigNum::from_u8` instead of returning `EINVAL`.
 
-**Fix.** Validate `value.exit_signal` without truncation: reject values that do not fit in `u8`, then call `SigNum::try_from` and propagate its `EINVAL`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding `validate-at-boundaries` comment: validate `value.exit_signal` without truncation, reject values that do not fit in `u8`, then call `SigNum::try_from` and propagate its `EINVAL`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 102
 
@@ -304,7 +306,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (major): `raw_flags` is a syscall argument, but `CloneArgs::for_clone` converts the low byte with `SigNum::from_u8` instead of validating it. A hostile caller can invoke `clone()` with an exit signal such as `65`; `SigNum::from_u8(65)` panics, turning invalid user input into a kernel panic instead of `EINVAL`.
 
-**Fix.** Validate the user-supplied signal at the syscall boundary and propagate `EINVAL`, for example by using `SigNum::try_from(exit_signal as u8)?` after checking `exit_signal != 0`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding Correctness comment: make `CloneArgs` construction validate exit signals with `SigNum::try_from` and propagate `EINVAL` before storing `Option<SigNum>`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 97
 
@@ -315,7 +317,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (major): `clone3()` accepts `flags` as a user-supplied `u64`, but this code narrows it with `value.flags as u32` before validation. A caller can set any high 32 flag bits and they are silently discarded, so invalid user input is accepted as a different internal `CloneFlags` value instead of being rejected at the syscall boundary.
 
-**Fix.** Reject any `flags` value that cannot be represented exactly as the supported `CloneFlags` bitmask before constructing `CloneFlags`, for example by checking the upper 32 bits and then using `CloneFlags::from_bits` on the validated value.
+**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding Correctness comment: reject any `flags` value that cannot be represented exactly as the supported `CloneFlags` bitmask before constructing `CloneFlags`, for example by checking the upper 32 bits and then using `CloneFlags::from_bits` on the validated value.
 
 ### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 100
 
@@ -326,7 +328,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (major): `exit_signal` comes directly from the user-provided `clone_args`, but `CloneArgs::try_from` truncates it to `u8` and calls `SigNum::from_u8`. Inputs such as `65` panic, and inputs such as `0x1_0000_0011` are silently accepted as `SIGCHLD`, so invalid user input is not validated at the syscall boundary.
 
-**Fix.** Require `value.exit_signal` to be either `0` or a valid signal number, and build it with `SigNum::try_from` instead of `SigNum::from_u8`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding Correctness comment: require `value.exit_signal` to be either `0` or a valid signal number, and build it with `SigNum::try_from` instead of `SigNum::from_u8`.

===== EXPECTED DEFECTS =====
# Expected defects

1. location: kernel/src/process/clone.rs lines 132-137 (persona: security, grounding: wrong location for `CLONE_FS` and `CLONE_NEWNS` checks, severity: major)
   defect: The `CLONE_FS` and `CLONE_NEWNS` incompatibility check is placed in `CloneArgs::for_clone`, which is only used by the legacy `clone` syscall. Both `clone` and `clone3` call `clone_child`, and `clone_child` invokes the common `CloneArgs::check`, but `clone3` constructs `CloneArgs` through `TryFrom<Clone3Args>` and bypasses `for_clone`. Consequently, invalid `clone3` flag combinations can reach the common clone implementation without this required validation.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should identify that validation in `for_clone` is not shared by `clone3` and require the `CLONE_FS`/namespace compatibility check to live in the common `CloneArgs::check` path.

2. location: kernel/src/process/clone.rs lines 185-225 (persona: security, grounding: imcomplete `CloneArgs::check`, severity: major)
   defect: `CloneArgs::check` validates `CLONE_VM` and `CLONE_SIGHAND` only inside the `CLONE_THREAD` branch. It therefore accepts `CLONE_SIGHAND` without `CLONE_VM`, although the Linux clone contract requires `CLONE_VM` whenever `CLONE_SIGHAND` is specified. This invalid flag combination is not rejected for either clone entry point once the common check is reached.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag the missing unconditional dependency check and require `CLONE_SIGHAND` without `CLONE_VM` to be rejected with `EINVAL`.

3. location: kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs lines 84-124 (persona: security, grounding: clone3() performs some additional checks, severity: major)
   defect: `TryFrom<Clone3Args>` converts `value.stack` directly and only converts `value.stack_size` to `Option<NonZeroU64>`. It accepts exactly one of the stack address and stack size, and it does not verify that the stack range lies in userspace. `clone3` can therefore pass an incomplete or invalid stack description into later child-context setup instead of returning `EINVAL`.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag the missing `clone3` stack validation and require `EINVAL` for a stack address without size, a size without address, or a stack range outside userspace.

4. location: kernel/src/process/exit.rs lines 49-73 (persona: development, grounding: race condition in find_reaper_process implementation, severity: major)
   defect: `find_reaper_process` keeps the current ancestor as a weak reference and walks upward by upgrading each parent. If the parent and grandparent exit and are reaped concurrently, the grandparent upgrade can fail. The loop then terminates and returns `None`, even though a current parent chain may still identify the correct reaper. The caller then falls back to adopting children into init, which is incorrect for this race.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag that a transient failed weak upgrade is treated as proof that no reaper exists and require retry logic for concurrent parent/grandparent exit and reaping.

5. location: kernel/src/thread/task.rs lines 54-62 (persona: development, grounding: memory-order issues for accessing is_child_subreaper and has_child_subreaper, severity: major)
   defect: Child startup checks only that `set_child_tid` is numerically within the userspace virtual-address range, then calls `write_val(...).unwrap()`. An address can be in that range but unmapped or unwritable, causing the user-memory write to return an error and the child task to panic. This is a user-controlled invalid pointer reaching a kernel `unwrap`.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag the reachable `unwrap()` after only a virtual address-range check and require invalid or unwritable `set_child_tid` addresses to be handled without a kernel panic.

# Summary

The process cloning code is structured around a useful common `CloneArgs` representation, and the review found several places where the implementation is already explicit about Linux flag constraints. The highest-risk issues are all at the syscall boundary: user-controlled signal numbers are converted with `SigNum::from_u8` and can panic, `clone3` truncates 64-bit flags before validation, unsupported `clone3` fields are accepted after a warning, and `CLONE_CHILD_SETTID` can unwrap an `EFAULT` in the child task entry path.

The next fixes should focus on making `CloneArgs` construction the single checked boundary for both `clone` and `clone3`, validating signals and flag widths before storing them, rejecting unsupported `clone3` fields until implemented, and propagating child TID write errors without panic. Structurally, `CloneArgs` should hide its fields behind checked constructors, and the Linux compatibility docs should be updated for the newly supported `CLONE_NEWNS` and `CLONE_PARENT` flag surface.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 106

> ```diff
> 106	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default)]
> 107	pub struct CloneArgs {
> ```

`alphabetical-attrs` (nit): `CloneArgs` derives `Debug`, `Clone`, `Copy`, and `Default` out of the required alphabetical order, which makes attribute diffs inconsistent with the local Rust-specific convention.

**Fix.** Sort the derived traits as `Clone`, `Copy`, `Debug`, `Default`.

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 108

> ```diff
> 106	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default)]
> 107	pub struct CloneArgs {
> 108	    pub flags: CloneFlags,
> 109	    pub pidfd: Option<Vaddr>,
> 110	    pub child_tid: Vaddr,
> 111	    pub parent_tid: Option<Vaddr>,
> 112	    pub exit_signal: Option<SigNum>,
> 113	    pub stack: u64,
> 114	    pub stack_size: Option<NonZeroU64>,
> 115	    pub tls: u64,
> 116	    pub _set_tid: Option<u64>,
> 117	    pub _set_tid_size: Option<u64>,
> 118	    pub _cgroup: Option<u64>,
> 119	}
> ```

`information-hiding` (major): `CloneArgs` is documented as an internal homogenized representation, but every field is `pub`, including unchecked ABI details such as `_set_tid`, `_set_tid_size`, and `_cgroup`. That exposes the representation to the whole crate and lets later call sites construct states that bypass the checked constructors.

**Fix.** Keep `CloneArgs` fields private and expose checked constructors or accessors. Move `TryFrom<Clone3Args>` into `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` or add a `CloneArgs::for_clone3(...)` constructor so `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` does not need field access.

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 130

> ```diff
> 130	        const FLAG_MASK: u64 = 0xff;
> 131	        let flags = CloneFlags::from(raw_flags {prompt} !FLAG_MASK);
> 139	        let exit_signal = raw_flags {prompt} FLAG_MASK;
> ```

`descriptive-names` (minor): `FLAG_MASK` does not say what is being masked. At the use sites, the reader has to know the `clone(2)` ABI detail that the low byte of `raw_flags` carries the exit signal.

**Fix.** Rename `FLAG_MASK` to a semantic name such as `EXIT_SIGNAL_MASK`, and use that name in both `flags` extraction and `exit_signal` extraction.

### `kernel/src/process/process/mod.rs` line 1

> ```diff
> 1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
> 2	
> 3	use core::{
> ```

`module-docs` (minor): `kernel/src/process/process/mod.rs` is the central `Process` module, but it starts with imports instead of a `//!` module comment explaining its purpose, key types such as `Process`, `ParentProcess`, and `ProcessGroup`, and how it relates to neighboring process modules.

**Fix.** Add a module-level `//!` block after the SPDX header that describes the process abstraction, parent/child tracking, process-group/session integration, and the main types exposed by the module.

### `kernel/src/process/process/mod.rs` line 45

> ```diff
> 45	/// Process id.
> 46	pub type Pid = u32;
> 51	/// Process group id.
> 52	pub type Pgid = u32;
> 53	/// Session Id.
> 54	pub type Sid = u32;
> ```

`rust-type-invariants` (major): `Pid`, `Pgid`, and `Sid` are all aliases of `u32`, so the type system cannot distinguish process IDs, process-group IDs, and session IDs at call sites such as `move_process_to_group({prompt}self, pid: Pid, pgid: Pgid)`.

**Fix.** Introduce small newtypes for `Pid`, `Pgid`, and `Sid`, with explicit conversions only where the Linux ABI requires raw `u32` values. Keep atomic wrappers around the newtypes or their raw representation.

### `kernel/src/process/process/mod.rs` line 136

> ```diff
> 134	    // Namespaces
> 135	    /// The user namespace
> 136	    #[expect(dead_code)]
> 137	    user_ns: Mutex<Arc<UserNamespace>>,
> ```

`expect-dead-code` (minor): `#[expect(dead_code)]` on `user_ns` does not state the concrete planned use that will make the field live, so the next reader cannot tell whether this is temporary namespace work or stale state.

**Fix.** Either add the missing consumer for `user_ns`, remove the field, or add a short comment next to `#[expect(dead_code)]` naming the concrete future use, such as planned `CLONE_NEWUSER` support.

### `kernel/src/process/process/mod.rs` line 686

> ```diff
> 684	    /// Returns the parent death signal.
> 685	    ///
> 686	    /// The parent death signal is the signal will be sent to child processes
> 687	    /// when the process exits.
> 688	    pub fn parent_death_signal({prompt}self) -> Option<SigNum> {
> ```

Misleading comment (minor): The doc comment for `parent_death_signal` says the signal is sent to child processes when this process exits, but the field comment above says it is the signal this process receives when its parent exits.

**Fix.** Rewrite the paragraph to describe the stored value from this process's perspective: `The parent death signal is sent to this process when its parent process exits.`

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 31

> ```diff
> 30	pub fn sys_clone3(
> 31	    clong_args_addr: Vaddr,
> 36	    trace!(
> 37	        "clone args addr = 0x{:x}, size = 0x{:x}",
> 38	        clong_args_addr,
> 46	        let args: Clone3Args = ctx.user_space().read_val(clong_args_addr)?;
> ```

`descriptive-names` (minor): `clong_args_addr` appears to be a typo for `clone_args_addr`, so every use site carries a spelling mistake instead of the syscall argument's meaning.

**Fix.** Rename `clong_args_addr` to `clone_args_addr` throughout `sys_clone3`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 42

> ```diff
> 41	    if size != size_of::<Clone3Args>() {
> 42	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid size");
> 43	    }
> ```

`error-message-format` (minor): The error message `invalid size` is too generic for `sys_clone3`; it does not identify `size` or the required `Clone3Args` size.

**Fix.** Make the message specific, for example: `` `size` must equal `size_of::<Clone3Args>()` ``.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 58

> ```diff
> 57	#[repr(C)]
> 58	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Pod)]
> 59	struct Clone3Args {
> ```

`alphabetical-attrs` (nit): `Clone3Args` derives `Debug`, `Clone`, `Copy`, and `Pod` out of alphabetical order.

**Fix.** Sort the derived traits as `Clone`, `Copy`, `Debug`, `Pod`.

### `kernel/src/thread/task.rs` line 24

> ```diff
> 24	/// create new task with userspace and parent process
> 25	pub fn create_new_user_task(
> ```

`rfc1574-summary` (nit): The first doc line for `create_new_user_task` is not a third-person sentence and starts with lowercase `create`.

**Fix.** Change the summary to a proper function doc sentence, such as `Creates a new user task with userspace state and a parent process.`

### `kernel/src/thread/task.rs` line 29

> ```diff
> 25	pub fn create_new_user_task(
> 26	    user_ctx: Box<UserContext>,
> 27	    thread_ref: Arc<Thread>,
> 28	    thread_local: ThreadLocal,
> 29	    is_init_process: bool,
> 74	        if is_init_process {
> 75	            crate::init_in_first_process({prompt}ctx);
> 76	        }
> ```

`no-bool-args` (minor): `is_init_process` is a boolean parameter that selects extra initialization behavior inside `create_new_user_task`, so the call site cannot show which task construction path is being requested without checking the implementation.

**Fix.** Split the behavior into separate constructors, such as `create_new_user_task(...)` and `create_new_init_user_task(...)`, or replace `is_init_process` with a typed enum like `UserTaskKind::{Regular, Init}`.

### `kernel/src/thread/task.rs` line 31

> ```diff
> 31	    let user_task_entry = move |user_ctx: UserContext| {
> 121	    let user_task_func = move || user_task_entry(*user_ctx);
> ```

`closure-fn-suffix` (nit): `user_task_entry` stores a closure but does not use the required `_fn` suffix. The same pattern appears again with `user_task_func`, which uses `func` instead of `_fn`.

**Fix.** Rename the closure variables to `user_task_entry_fn` and `user_task_fn`.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 163

> ```diff
> 139	let exit_signal = raw_flags {prompt} FLAG_MASK;
> ...
> 163	exit_signal: (exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(exit_signal as u8)),
> ```

Reachable panic (critical): `CloneArgs::for_clone` converts the user-controlled low byte of `raw_flags` with `SigNum::from_u8`. A call such as `clone(0xff, ...)` reaches the `panic!` in `SigNum::from_u8` instead of returning `EINVAL`, so an invalid exit signal can panic the kernel.

**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding `validate-at-boundaries` comment: make `CloneArgs` construction validate exit signals with `SigNum::try_from` and propagate `EINVAL` before storing `Option<SigNum>`.

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 579

> ```diff
> 576	if new_sp != 0 {
> 577	    // If stack size is not 0, the `new_sp` points to the BOTTOMMOST byte of stack.
> 578	    if let Some(size) = stack_size {
> 579	        child_context.set_stack_pointer((new_sp + size.get()) as usize)
> 580	    }
> ```

`checked-arithmetic` (major): `clone_user_ctx` adds the user-provided `new_sp` and `stack_size` with unchecked arithmetic. With `clone3({ stack: u64::MAX - 3, stack_size: 8, ... })`, the stack pointer wraps to a low address in release builds instead of rejecting the invalid stack range.

**Fix.** Use `checked_add` for `new_sp + size.get()` and return `EOVERFLOW` or `EINVAL` on overflow. That likely means making `clone_user_ctx` return `Result<UserContext>` and propagating the error at its call sites.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 89

> ```diff
> 88	// TODO: Deal with set_tid, set_tid_size, cgroup
> 89	if value.set_tid != 0 || value.set_tid_size != 0 {
> 90	    warn!("set_tid is not supported");
> 91	}
> 92	
> 93	if value.cgroup != 0 {
> 94	    warn!("cgroup is not supported");
> 95	}
> ```

Unsupported request ignored (major): `clone3` only logs nonzero `set_tid`, `set_tid_size`, and `cgroup`, then continues. A caller requesting `set_tid_size = 1` gets a child with an arbitrary allocated PID, and a caller passing `cgroup` gets a child outside the requested cgroup while the syscall still reports success.

**Fix.** Until these fields are implemented, reject nonzero `set_tid`, `set_tid_size`, or `cgroup` with an error such as `EINVAL` instead of silently ignoring the requested semantics.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 97

> ```diff
> 97	let flags = CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)
> 98	    .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid clone flags"))?;
> ```

Invalid flags accepted (major): `CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)` truncates the upper 32 bits of the `clone3` `flags` field before validation. For example, `flags = 0x1_0000_0000` is accepted as `CloneFlags::empty()` and creates a child instead of rejecting the unknown flag.

**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding `validate-at-boundaries` comment: reject nonzero upper bits before converting, for example with `u32::try_from(value.flags)` mapped to `EINVAL`, then pass the checked `u32` to `CloneFlags::from_bits`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 100

> ```diff
> 99	let exit_signal =
> 100	    (value.exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(value.exit_signal as u8));
> ```

Reachable panic (critical): `CloneArgs::try_from` casts the user-controlled `clone3` `exit_signal` to `u8` and calls `SigNum::from_u8`. An input such as `exit_signal = 256` is nonzero, truncates to `0`, and reaches the `panic!` in `SigNum::from_u8` instead of returning `EINVAL`.

**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding `validate-at-boundaries` comment: validate `value.exit_signal` without truncation, reject values that do not fit in `u8`, then call `SigNum::try_from` and propagate its `EINVAL`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 102

> ```diff
> 97	let flags = CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)
> 98	    .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid clone flags"))?;
> 99	let exit_signal =
> 100	    (value.exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(value.exit_signal as u8));
> 101	
> 102	if flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT | CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD)
> 103	    {prompt}{prompt} exit_signal.is_some()
> ```

Invalid flag combination accepted (major): `clone3` does not reject `CLONE_NEWNS | CLONE_FS`, although `CloneArgs::for_clone` rejects the same combination for `clone`. With both flags set, `clone_fs` shares the caller's `ThreadFsInfo`, then the `CLONE_NEWNS` path calls `switch_to_mnt_ns` through that shared object, so a successful privileged `clone3` can switch the parent's filesystem resolver too.

**Fix.** Add the same `CLONE_NEWNS | CLONE_FS` validation to `CloneArgs::try_from` or to `CloneArgs::check`, returning `EINVAL` before any child state is created.

### `kernel/src/thread/task.rs` line 61

> ```diff
> 54	// The `clone` syscall may require child process to write the thread pid to the specified address.
> 55	// Make sure the store operation completes before the clone call returns control to user space
> 56	// in the child process.
> 57	let child_tid_ptr = current_thread_local.set_child_tid().get();
> 58	if is_userspace_vaddr(child_tid_ptr) {
> 59	    current_userspace!()
> 60	        .write_val(child_tid_ptr, {prompt}current_posix_thread.tid())
> 61	        .unwrap();
> 62	}
> ```

`propagate-errors` (critical): `CLONE_CHILD_SETTID` stores a user-provided pointer in `set_child_tid`, and the child task later writes through it with `.unwrap()`. A pointer that is in the userspace range but unmapped or read-only makes `write_val` return an error, so `clone(CLONE_CHILD_SETTID, child_tidptr = bad_addr, ...)` can panic the kernel in the child entry path.

**Fix.** Handle the `write_val` error explicitly instead of unwrapping. Since this runs after the child task starts, either arrange for the checked write to happen before scheduling the child, or terminate/signal the child on `EFAULT` according to the intended Linux-compatible behavior.

## Security

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 163

> ```diff
>     139        let exit_signal = raw_flags {prompt} FLAG_MASK;
> ...
>     163            exit_signal: (exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(exit_signal as u8)),
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `raw_flags` is a syscall argument, but `CloneArgs::for_clone` converts the low byte with `SigNum::from_u8` instead of validating it. A hostile caller can invoke `clone()` with an exit signal such as `65`; `SigNum::from_u8(65)` panics, turning invalid user input into a kernel panic instead of `EINVAL`.

**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding Correctness comment: make `CloneArgs` construction validate exit signals with `SigNum::try_from` and propagate `EINVAL` before storing `Option<SigNum>`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 97

> ```diff
>      97        let flags = CloneFlags::from_bits(value.flags as u32)
>      98            .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid clone flags"))?;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `clone3()` accepts `flags` as a user-supplied `u64`, but this code narrows it with `value.flags as u32` before validation. A caller can set any high 32 flag bits and they are silently discarded, so invalid user input is accepted as a different internal `CloneFlags` value instead of being rejected at the syscall boundary.

**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding Correctness comment: reject any `flags` value that cannot be represented exactly as the supported `CloneFlags` bitmask before constructing `CloneFlags`, for example by checking the upper 32 bits and then using `CloneFlags::from_bits` on the validated value.

### `kernel/src/syscall/clone.rs` line 100

> ```diff
>      99        let exit_signal =
>     100            (value.exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(value.exit_signal as u8));
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `exit_signal` comes directly from the user-provided `clone_args`, but `CloneArgs::try_from` truncates it to `u8` and calls `SigNum::from_u8`. Inputs such as `65` panic, and inputs such as `0x1_0000_0011` are silently accepted as `SIGCHLD`, so invalid user input is not validated at the syscall boundary.

**Fix.** Shared with the corresponding Correctness comment: require `value.exit_signal` to be either `0` or a valid signal number, and build it with `SigNum::try_from` instead of `SigNum::from_u8`.

## Documentation

### `kernel/src/process/clone.rs` line 251

> ```diff
> 239         let supported_flags = CloneFlags::CLONE_VM
> 240             | CloneFlags::CLONE_FS
> 241             | CloneFlags::CLONE_FILES
> 242             | CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND
> 243             | CloneFlags::CLONE_PIDFD
> 244             | CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD
> 245             | CloneFlags::CLONE_SYSVSEM
> 246             | CloneFlags::CLONE_SETTLS
> 247             | CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT_SETTID
> 248             | CloneFlags::CLONE_CHILD_SETTID
> 249             | CloneFlags::CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID
> 250             | CloneFlags::CLONE_VFORK
> 251             | CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS
> 252             | CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT;
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): `CloneFlags::check_unsupported_flags()` now treats `CLONE_NEWNS` as supported for `clone` and `clone3`, which enhances the user-visible syscall flag surface, but the Linux Compatibility docs in `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/` do not document this newly supported namespace flag or its constraints such as the `CLONE_NEWNS` plus `CLONE_FS` rejection.

**Fix.** Update the Linux Compatibility syscall coverage for `clone` and `clone3` in the same change, including the relevant Markdown coverage page and SCML coverage data, to list `CLONE_NEWNS` as supported and document the unsupported/invalid combinations.

**Fix.** Update the Linux Compatibility syscall coverage for `clone` and `clone3`, including the matching SCML coverage, to include `CLONE_PARENT` and its invalid combinations/limitations.
